* `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/ndm_reset/ndm_reset.rdl` ("NDMRESET_REQUEST", "NDMRESET_PROCESS", "NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT") is the source of the register behaviour: request bits below the cluster count follow the input and bits at and above it read 0, process bits below the count are driven on the output and the others are stored and not driven, and `NDMRESET_REQUEST` and `NDMRESET_PROCESS` reset to 0; `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` has the RDL reset value 0x0 and is written by hardware, so it reads the cluster count.
* `doc/integrator/src/smu-smc.adoc` ("External CPU cluster NDM reset") and `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` (`smc_ndmreset_request_i`, `smc_ndmreset_process_o`) are the source of the cluster count of 4 for this harness and of the bit-per-cluster port mapping.
* Request lane 0 and lane 3 are each driven high and then low; `NDMRESET_REQUEST` must read that single bit and then 0, and with all four request pins high it must read 0xF.
* `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` must read 4; after a cold reset with the request pins low `NDMRESET_REQUEST` must read 0, and it must then read 0x1 with request pin 0 driven high again.
* Process bit 0 and bit 3 are each written and cleared; `smc_ndmreset_process_o` must show that bit alone and then 0.
* `NDMRESET_PROCESS` written 0xFFFFFFF0 must read back 0xFFFFFFF0 with the port at 0, and written 0xFFFFFFFF must read back 0xFFFFFFFF with the port at 0xF.
* After a cold reset `NDMRESET_PROCESS` must read its RDL reset value and the port must read 0.
* The number of CSR reads before a request bit settles is bounded by the leaf and not measured as a latency; the synchronizer delay of the request input is not specified and is not checked.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the bench reset, open the TAP, require the SMC JTAG2AXI path to be enabled and drive all request pins low.
. For lane 0 and lane 3 in turn, drive the request pin high and read `NDMRESET_REQUEST` until the bit reads 1, requiring that single bit; drive it low and read until the bit reads 0, requiring 0.
. Drive all four request pins high, read until every lane reads 1 and require `NDMRESET_REQUEST` to read 0xF.
. Read `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` and require 4.
. Drive the request pins low, run a cold reset and require `NDMRESET_REQUEST` to read 0; drive request pin 0 high, read until bit 0 reads 1 and require 0x1, then drive it low and read until bit 0 reads 0.
. For lane 0 and lane 3 in turn, write the process bit, sample the port until the bit reads 1 and require that single bit; write 0, sample until the bit reads 0 and require 0.
. Write `NDMRESET_PROCESS` 0xFFFFFFF0 then 0xFFFFFFFF; after each write require the read back to equal the written value and the port to settle at 0x0 then 0xF.
. Run a cold reset and require `NDMRESET_PROCESS` to read its reset value and the port to read 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-NDM-REQUEST-S1` |`NDMRESET_REQUEST` follows request pin 0 and pin 3 high and low.
|`CHK-NDM-REQUEST-S2` |`NDMRESET_REQUEST` reads 0xF with all four request pins high.
|`CHK-NDM-REQUEST-S3` |`NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` reads 4.
|`CHK-NDM-REQUEST-S4` |`NDMRESET_REQUEST` reads 0 with all four request pins low after a cold reset and follows request pin 0 high again.
|`CHK-NDM-PROCESS-S1` |`NDMRESET_PROCESS` bit 0 and bit 3 each drive only their own process lane.
|`CHK-NDM-PROCESS-S2` |`NDMRESET_PROCESS` stores 32 bits and only bits 3:0 reach the port.
|`CHK-NDM-PROCESS-S3` |`NDMRESET_PROCESS` and the process port read 0 after a cold reset.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-137-smu_reset_csr_access_test]]
===== 137 `smu_reset_csr_access_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_reset_csr_access_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_reset_csr_access_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` on the DTP TAP with the SMC JTAG2AXI frontdoor (`tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read low before any access); TB pins `tb_cool_reset_pin`, `tb_gpio_drive_en` / `tb_gpio_drive_val` bit 53 (isolation pin, driven low), `tb_sync_irq` (observes `sync_irq_o`) and `tb_smc_ndmreset_request` left low; observation of `obs_smc_rst_n_o`; the SMC reset-unit registers (`SS_CONFIG`, `SS_CONFIG_LOCK`, `SS_WARM_RESET_N`, `SS_RESET_COMPLETE`, `SYNC_REG`, the isolation registers, `ISOLATE_REQ_VIS` and both FLR counters) and the NDM registers `NDMRESET_REQUEST`, `NDMRESET_PROCESS` and `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT`; the unmapped reset-unit offsets 0x28, 0x2C, 0x3C and 0xCC; `RANDOM_SEED` for the random data patterns; `clk_ref_i` and `clk_smu_i`; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The reset-unit and NDM registers read their reset values after reset, accept and mask random data where they are writable, keep their value under writes where they are read-only or unmapped, and `SYNC_REG.sync` drives `sync_irq_o`.

*Test Coverage*:

* `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/reset_unit/reset_unit.rdl` and `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/ndm_reset/ndm_reset.rdl` are the source of every reset value and access type compared here: the isolation registers, both FLR counters, `NDMRESET_REQUEST`, `NDMRESET_PROCESS` and `SYNC_REG` read 0, and `ISOLATE_REQ_VIS` reads 1 on bits 4 and 8, with bit 0 following the isolation pin.
* `doc/integrator/src/smu-smc.adoc` ("External CPU cluster NDM reset") is the source of `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` reading 4 for this harness; `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` describes `sync_irq_o` as the output of `SYNC_REG.sync`.
* `SS_CONFIG`, `SS_WARM_RESET_N`, `NDMRESET_PROCESS` and `ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_COUNTER_VALUE` are written with random 32-bit data (and `SS_CONFIG` also with a single random field bit) and must read back the data masked to the register field; each is restored to its reset value afterwards.
* `SS_RESET_COMPLETE`, `ISOLATE_REQ_VIS`, `NDMRESET_REQUEST` and `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` are written with all ones (and `SS_RESET_COMPLETE` with the inverse of its value as well) and must read the value they held before the write.
* Reads and writes at the offsets 0x28, 0x2C, 0x3C and 0xCC, where `reset_unit.rdl` places no register, must leave every defined reset-unit register at the value it held before.
* The AXI response of each write to a read-only register and of each read and write at an unmapped offset is logged as measured; the specification does not state it, so the leaf does not compare it.
* `SYNC_REG.sync` written 1 must raise `sync_irq_o` and written 0 must lower it; after a primary reset with the bit set, `SYNC_REG` must read 0 and `sync_irq_o` must read 0 (`hw/sys/smc/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, "Reset Architecture": the reset-unit registers are reset by the primary reset).
* The value of `SS_RESET_COMPLETE` is read before the write and not compared against a reset value.
* On this 2-state bench a missing reset of a zero-reset register is not observable here; the cold-reset clear from a programmed state is proven by `smu_isolation_retention_test`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the bench reset, open the TAP, require the SMC JTAG2AXI path to be enabled and drive GPIO 53 low.
. Read the four isolation registers and both FLR counters and require each to read its reset value.
. Read `NDMRESET_REQUEST`, `NDMRESET_PROCESS` and `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` with the request pins low and require 0, 0 and 4.
. Read `SYNC_REG` and require 0, then read `ISOLATE_REQ_VIS` and require bit 0 to equal the pin level and bits 4 and 8 to read 1.
. Read `SS_CONFIG_LOCK` and require it unlocked; write random data to each writable register, read it back, require the masked data, and restore the reset value.
. Write all ones to the read-only registers (and the inverse to `SS_RESET_COMPLETE`), log each AXI response and require each register to read its earlier value.
. Read every defined reset-unit register, read and write the four unmapped offsets with the responses logged, read the defined registers again and require each to be unchanged.
. Write `SYNC_REG` 1 and require `sync_irq_o` to read 1, write 0 and require it to read 0.
. Write `SYNC_REG` 1 and require `sync_irq_o` to read 1, run a cool reset, then require `SYNC_REG` and `sync_irq_o` to read 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CSR-RESET-VALUES-S2` |The isolation registers and both FLR counters read their reset values after the bring-up cold reset.
|`CHK-CSR-RESET-VALUES-S3` |`NDMRESET_REQUEST` and `NDMRESET_PROCESS` read 0 and `NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT` reads 4.
|`CHK-CSR-RESET-VALUES-S4` |`SYNC_REG` reads 0 and `ISOLATE_REQ_VIS` reads the pin level on bit 0 and 1 on bits 4 and 8.
|`CHK-CSR-ACCESS-S1` |Random data reads back masked to the register field from `SS_CONFIG`, `SS_WARM_RESET_N`, `NDMRESET_PROCESS` and `ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_COUNTER_VALUE`.
|`CHK-CSR-ACCESS-S2` |Read-only registers keep their value after a write of all ones.
|`CHK-CSR-ACCESS-S3` |Defined reset-unit registers keep their value across accesses to unmapped offsets.
|`CHK-SYNC-IRQ-S1` |`sync_irq_o` follows `SYNC_REG.sync` written 1 then 0.
|`CHK-SYNC-IRQ-S2` |`SYNC_REG` and `sync_irq_o` read 0 after a primary reset.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
==== MISC

[[sec-034-smu_clock_stop_coordination_test]]
===== 34 `smu_clock_stop_coordination_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_clock_stop_coordination_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: SMU_ALL_006: DTP boot-stall / IC-RESET / clock-stop aggregation.

*Test Coverage*:

* Scenarios: DTP-BOOT-STALL.S1/S2, DTP-IC-RESET.S1/S3, DTP-CLKSTOP-AGG.S1/S2/S3; no Force or deposit.
* DTP-FEAT-GATE.* and INT-FEAT-CTRL-DTP-GATE are owned by SMU_ALL_008 — out of scope.
* DTP-CLKSTOP-AGG.S3 attests only the reserved-port property: with `cla_clock_stop_en` set, the TB clock-stop request pins land on DTP[8:1] and leave DTP[0] unchanged. DTP[0] is the SMC's `clocks_stopped_by_cla`, which this bench cannot provoke (default SMC ROM, no CSR path to the CLA event), so its level is logged and no SMC-to-DTP connectivity is claimed for it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run without `+skip_fuse_sense` (the `testlists/all.toml` entry carries none): the SMC eFuse
  controller senses the `+smc_efuse_hex` image through the wrapper's eFuse model, and
  `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` is that controller's own sense-complete release. The run log
  carries `Not skipping fuse sense` and no shadow-preload load.
. Drive `smu_clock_stop_coordination_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Watch `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` 0 -> 1 after every primary reset (bounded at 5000 `clk_smu`; a
  flag already high, or an expiry, fails) before any `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` compare. At
  bring-up the release must then follow within 256 `clk_smu`. With the stall held across the cold
  reset the gate must read `0` on every one of the 64 `clk_smu` after that rise, so the `0` is the
  gate and not a sense still in progress. After the stall clears, with the sense already done, the
  release must arrive within 256 `clk_smu` (the `prim_sync3` + sticky flop + 16-stage pipe path,
  not the sense latency) and stay released for 64 `clk_smu`.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DTP-BOOT-STALL-S1` |`ovrd=1 stall=1` across cold reset holds the sensed `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` at 0 for 64 `clk_smu` after `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` rises for that cold reset
|`CHK-DTP-BOOT-STALL-S2` |clearing stall and override, with the sense already done, opens the gate within 256 `clk_smu` and it stays open for 64 `clk_smu`
|`CHK-DTP-IC-RESET-S1` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-DTP-IC-RESET-S3` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-DTP-CLKSTOP-AGG-S1` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-DTP-CLKSTOP-AGG-S2` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-DTP-CLKSTOP-AGG-S3` |DTP[0] unchanged by the TB clock-stop pins, DTP[8:1] follows them, `dtp_cla_clock_stop_en` set and cleared as programmed
|`CHK-NONVAC` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-035-smu_i3c_mem_port_connectivity_test]]
===== 35 `smu_i3c_mem_port_connectivity_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_i3c_mem_port_connectivity_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: FAB_SMC_031 delivery-only via J2A; no Force / no sep_in.

*Test Coverage*:

* FAB_SMC_031 — AXI4-Lite local peripheral delivery (UART/I2C/GPIO/I3C/AVSBus).
* Scope on the SEP=1 wrapper (no sep_in, no Force): S1 J2A read at each peripheral destination; status must not be DECERR.
* Delivery is driven over the JTAG2AXI frontdoor to the PeakRDL-generated destinations (delivery only; no field semantics).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_i3c_mem_port_connectivity_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SUB-AXIL-LOCAL-J2A-READY` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-SUB-AXIL-LOCAL-S1` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|assertion |i3c_mem incomplete s1=<value> observed=<value>
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-036-smu_jtag2axi_vs_smn_same_csr_race_test]]
===== 36 `smu_jtag2axi_vs_smn_same_csr_race_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_jtag2axi_vs_smn_same_csr_race_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: Race J2A vs SMN on SCRATCH with the overlap established from handshakes; coherent winner, no tear.

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI vs SMN concurrent write on the same CSR; no tear.
* The SMN writer is released only after the J2A write's AW handshake is seen on the DTP -> SMC debug port, and the J2A AW/B handshakes there plus the SMN AW/B handshakes at the SMU boundary are timestamped; the race is accepted only when the SMN AW was accepted before the J2A B returned, so both writes were outstanding in the SMC at once.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_jtag2axi_vs_smn_same_csr_race_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-J2ASMN-GATE-OPEN` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-J2ASMN-CLEAR` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-J2ASMN-WR-J` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-J2ASMN-WR-S` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-J2ASMN-OVERLAP` |SMN AW accepted inside the J2A write's AW..B window at the debug port
|`CHK-RACE-J2A-SMN` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-J2ASMN-VERSION` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|assertion |jtag2axi_vs_smn race incomplete s1=<value> s2=<value>
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-037-smu_system_timer_octs_test]]
===== 37 `smu_system_timer_octs_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_system_timer_octs_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: SYS-TIMER-OCTS via J2A + tb_timer_count; no Force / no sep_in.

*Test Coverage*:

* SYS-TIMER-OCTS: primary strap + free-run COUNT via product pin.
* S1: SMC_ATTRIBUTES.chiplet_is_primary vs tb_top hardwire.
* S2: PRESET + TIMER_START → STATUS.RUNNING; tb_timer_count advances.
* S3: Larger PRESET + START; pin reloads to the new PRESET then advances.
* S4: TIMER_PRESET_HI/LO is a read-write 64-bit preset (generated `system_timer_octs` description), so START loads it whole: a preset with every bit from 13 up set, 8192 counts short of the wrap, then a preset of zero, each followed by START, put `timer_count_o` at the preset within the reload slack, so bits 13..63 rise and fall on the pin. The count then free-runs from zero, and `timer_count_o` updates every cycle while the timer runs (`hw/ip/system_timer_octs/doc/interface.adoc`); it is sampled every clock until each of bits 0..12 has been seen at 0 and at 1.
* TIMER_COUNT_{HI,LO} is read over J2A and required to land between two samples of the product pin `tb_timer_count` (`timer_count_o`) taken either side of that read, so the CSR view and the pin view of the same counter are compared. The bracket is as tight as the J2A read latency allows; a COUNT CSR left at its reset value, or one aliasing another register, falls below the lower sample.
* 32-bit CSRs at addr[2]=1 use the upper 64b J2A lane (wstrb=0xF0), matching `wdt_unlock`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_system_timer_octs_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OCTS-J2A-READY` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OCTS-PRIMARY-STRAP` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OCTS-COUNT-MONOTONIC` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OCTS-CSR-COUNT` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OCTS-PRESET-RELOAD` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OCTS-PRESET-RELOAD-ADVANCE` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OCTS-PRESET-EXTREMES` |after each extreme PRESET and START, `tb_timer_count` sits between the preset and the preset plus the reload slack, and in the free run after the zero preset each of its bits 0..12 is seen at 0 and at 1
|assertion |octs incomplete s1=<value> s2=<value> s3=<value> s4=<value>
|assertion |tb_timer_count pin observe missing or not advancing
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-038-smu_xtrig_ctm_illegal_phase_test]]
===== 38 `smu_xtrig_ctm_illegal_phase_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_xtrig_ctm_illegal_phase_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: Illegal CTM: abort + double-req on product pins with a live ack control; SMC[1:0] clean.

*Test Coverage*:

* live control: lane 7 is the one lane both TB tops configure in req/ack mode (`XTRIG_INT_CT_MODE = 8'h80`; per the Integrator Guide cross-trigger port section a mode bit of 1 uses the ack, 0 is pulse-synchronisation with no ack). A request on lane 7 must raise `xtrig_ctm_dst_ack[7]` within the settle window and hold it, and releasing the request must clear it, so the "ack stays 0" legs below measure a path shown alive in the same run.
* req drop without ack: assert then clear dst_req on pulse-sync lanes -> TB ack stays 0; SMC[1:0] idle (no phantom handshake)
* double-req: change pattern mid-req without completing four-phase -> new pattern visible on DTP[9:2]; SMC[1:0] never polluted

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run the test body `cocotb_wrapper/tests/smu_xtrig_ctm_illegal_phase_test.py` over the wrapper's product interfaces; it drives the TAP and pins directly and has no separate sequence file.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-XT-ILLEGAL` |Evidence token `XT_ILLEGAL_PHASE`: lane-7 ack rises and falls with its request, then DTP[9:2] follows abort/double-req pin patterns with the ack held at 0
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-039-smu_boundary_port_composition_test]]
===== 39 `smu_boundary_port_composition_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_boundary_port_composition_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `elaboration` firmware mode; SMC eFuse image `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/smc_efuse_all_ones.hex`, sensed by the SMC eFuse controller (no `+skip_fuse_sense`)

*Objective*: The SEP=1 SMU boundary ports are present at the widths the specifications state, the SMC-side ID-width converter presents the 6-bit subsystem ID the SMC system AXI input specifies, `ss_config_o` and `skip_mem_repair_o` present their specified idle values, and the CTP channels stay inert while their data inputs rest.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every port read has a row in `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`, which gives presence, direction, type, width expression and, for the CTP channels, the "Tie to '0 if unused" note the inertness legs rest on.
* Every width compare takes its expected value from a specification: the port table's literal widths (`lcc_demote_state_*_o` `[1:0]`, `ss_reset_ctrl_o` `[31:0]`, `skip_mem_repair_o` 1); `lc_state_o` as 8 bits (port table tie value `8'hf0`; `hw/sys/smc/doc/port_table.adoc` `lc_state_i` "(8 bits)"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` life-cycle state width 4); `XTRIG_NUM_CTP` = 16 from `doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` "SMU Default Parameters"; 256 external interrupts from `hw/sys/smc/doc/port_table.adoc` `smc_ext_interrupts_i`; the 6-bit ID from that table's `sys_axi_in_req_i` row and `doc/integrator/src/smu-smc.adoc` "AXI Interface Configuration"; the `SS_CONFIG` width and reset value from the generated `hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/c/blocks/reset_unit.h`. `cocotb/seq_lib/smu_compose_helpers.py` names the source of each constant.
* The SMN request/response struct widths, the crossbar-side ID width of both ID-width converters and the SEP-side ID width have no specified value. They are logged as observations and not compared.
* The idle-value compares read an output after bring-up against the value the specification gives for that state: `ss_config_o` equals the `SS_CONFIG` reset value, and `skip_mem_repair_o` is clear while no isolation request is pending (`smu.adoc`, memory repair: a cold reset without isolation executes repair), with the wrapper FLR pin sampled low as the precondition. Each can fail on a wrong idle value; neither distinguishes an undriven output from one driven to that value. Write-through of `SS_CONFIG` to `ss_config_o` and the FLR path onto `skip_mem_repair_o` are proven by `smu_smc_boundary_io_test`.
* `smc_shadow_regs_o`: `port_table.adoc` types it `smc_efuse_pkg::efuse_map_t` and states no width, so the `smu_wrapper` port and the bench net are compared at width with the `smu` port. After `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` rises for the cold boot, the `smu` port, the `smu_wrapper` port and the bench net each carry the image `+smc_efuse_hex` names, fuse word `i` at shadow bits `32i+31..32i`; the image programs every bit, and the leaf requires it to program at least one, so a disconnected or narrowed wrapper port reads a zero where the image has a one.
* The CTP data inputs are read at their resting level at the SMU boundary and at the DTP consumer. `smu_wrapper` passes them through; the bench, as the integrator the port table's "Tie to '0 if unused" note addresses, holds `req_in`, `ack_in` and `ack_out` at zero (`tb/tb_wrapper_top.sv` ties `ack_out`, and `req_in` and `ack_in` follow bench nets `smu_base_test` drives to zero; stand-in entry "CTP data-input tie-offs" in `hw/sys/smu/dv/docs/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`), and `req_out` sits on the bench's wire-OR board and rests at all ones, the pull-up of the active-low wire the generated header's `CONFIG.INVERT` reset of 0 selects, with no chiplet pulling. Every CTP output and the CTM boundary must then stay static at zero over the inert window; that leg is device-observed. A cross-trigger block held in reset, unclocked or disconnected would also stay static, so `smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test` (<<sec-063-smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test>>) is the live control for this leg: on the same bench a request on `xtrig_ctp_req_in_din_i[0]` raises `xtrig_ctp_ack_out_dout_o[0]` at the pads.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read each named port on the elaborated `smu` instance for its presence and its specified width.
. Read the subsystem-side ID width of the SMC ID-width converter and compare it with the specified 6-bit SMC system AXI input ID; log the SMN struct widths and the two converters' crossbar-side and SEP-side ID widths without comparing them.
. Sample the CTP data inputs at the SMU boundary and the DTP consumer and require their resting levels, then require every CTP output and the CTM boundary static at zero over the inert window.
. Sample `ss_config_o` and `skip_mem_repair_o` after bring-up and compare them with their specified idle values.
. Compare the widths of the `smu_wrapper` `smc_shadow_regs_o` port and the bench net with the `smu` port, wait for `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` to rise, and compare all three with the image.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-EXT-SMN-S4` |the SMC-side ID-width converter presents the specified 6-bit subsystem ID
|`CHK-SMU-INT-AGG-S1` |smc_ext_interrupts_i is 256 wide, the SMC external interrupt count
|`CHK-SMU-XTRIG-CTP-S1` |all four CTP groups are 16 wide, the XTRIG_NUM_CTP default
|`CHK-SMU-XTRIG-CTP-S6` |bench-held resting CTP data inputs leave the CTP outputs static (live control: `smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test`)
|`CHK-SMU-LC-STATE-S1` |lc_state_o is 8 bits, 2*LcStateWidth
|`CHK-SMU-LC-DEMOTE-S1` |both lcc_demote_state outputs are 2 bits
|`CHK-SMU-EFUSE-SHIM-SMC-S3` |the smu_wrapper smc_shadow_regs_o port and the bench net are as wide as the smu port, and after the sense completes all three carry the non-zero image +smc_efuse_hex names
|`CHK-SMU-FUSE-SENSE-S4` |skip_mem_repair_o is one bit and clear with no isolation request pending
|`CHK-SMU-SSRESET-S2` |ss_reset_ctrl_o has 32 elements
|`CHK-SMU-SSRESET-S4` |ss_config_o is 32 bits and presents the SS_CONFIG reset value
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-040-smu_composition_parameter_test]]
===== 40 `smu_composition_parameter_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_composition_parameter_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `elaboration` firmware mode, `+expected_sep=1`, `+xtrig_int_ct_mode=0x80`; the bench sets the `CFG` field `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to the SHA-256 of the all-zero 32-byte token (`tb_wrapper_top.sv`), standing in for the metal digest so that a frontdoor token can take the match

*Objective*: The build parameters the wrapper passes down reach the instances that consume them at the values the specification states.

*Test Coverage*:

* The goldens are specification values. `doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` "SMU Default Parameters" and "Cross Trigger Parameters": 16 external CTPs, 8 exposed internal CT lanes and 8 exposed clock-stop requests, with the DTP carrying 10 and 9 once the SMC reservation is added ("Debug & Test Ports (DTP) Integration"); `SMC_OTP_*_PL_DEPTH` `2'h3`; `JTAG_NUM_EXTRA_STAPS` 1 with the port array as wide as the parameter. "Pipeline Depth Parameters": `SEP_OTP_*_PL_DEPTH` fixed to `2'h3`. `hw/sys/smc/doc/port_table.adoc`: 256 external interrupts. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: the 256-bit token. `+xtrig_int_ct_mode` is the bench's declared elaboration input; the DTP is required to present it above zeroed SMC-reserved bits ("SMC-reserved DTP mode bits are 0 (pulse-sync)").
* Plumbing compares read one parameter at the wrapper and at the instance that consumes it: `CFG` at the wrapper and at `smu`.
* The token the SEP eFuse controller carries is compared against the SHA-256 of the all-zero 32-byte token, computed in the sequence, which is the value the bench binds in `CFG` in place of the metal digest; the product default is not what this composition elaborates.
* The packed layout of the build configuration struct is unspecified, so `CFG` is not decoded field by field: it is compared whole between the wrapper and `smu`, and each parameter is proven at the instance that consumes it. `SEP` is proven by `sep_enabled_o`, the token by the SEP eFuse controller's parameter; `SEP_KM_LATCHED_MEM_RDATA`, `EXT_TRNG_NUM_AXIS`, `MAX_TRANS`, the SMC `RD`/`WR` pipeline depths, the eFuse shim sizes and the ABR parameters have no consumer compare in this leaf.
* The security_disable net is constant zero while no token is written, so this leaf only records it at the SEP eFuse controller that drives it, the `smu` wire and the SMC input, untokened. `smu_sep_sba_peripheral_test` S6 carries the claim: it reads the same three nodes at 0 before the token and at 1 after the matching token (`CHK-SEP-SECURITY-DISABLE`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the DTP SEP OTP pipeline depth parameters at the instance and compare against the specified fixed depth.
. Require the wrapper's `CFG` to reach `smu` unchanged and log its width and value.
. Read each consumer parameter (`u_dtp.XTRIG_NUM_CTP`, `XTRIG_NUM_INT_CT`, `XTRIG_NUM_CLK_STOP_REQ`, `XTRIG_INT_CT_MODE` and its width, `SMC_OTP_*_PL_DEPTH`; the `smc_ext_interrupts_i` and `jtag_stap_extra_host_tdi_i` port widths) and compare against the specified value.
. Read SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN at the SEP eFuse controller; require the specified 256-bit width and the digest the bench binds in `CFG`.
. Record security_disable at the SEP eFuse controller's output, the `smu` wire and the SMC input.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-SEC-TOKEN-S2` |SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN is 256 bits at the SEP eFuse controller and is the digest the bench binds in CFG
|`CHK-SMU-OTPAXI-SEP-S3` |DTP SEP OTP pipeline depths are the specified fixed 3
|`CHK-SMU-NOSEP-S4` |CFG reaches smu unchanged and each consumer parameter read at its instance is its specified default, plus the SMC reservation where the specification adds one
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-041-smu_smc_boundary_io_test]]
===== 41 `smu_smc_boundary_io_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_boundary_io_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the CSR reads and writes); TB pin drive; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The SMC-facing wrapper boundary signals that `port_table.adoc` declares are proved through the SMC register map or, for the external interrupts, the SMC CPU interrupt vector `interrupts.adoc` defines, each leg comparing a DUT-produced value against the contract.

*Test Coverage*:

* The NDM reset request and process pair, an external interrupt lane, the reset-unit terminations (SS_CONFIG, SYNC_REG, ISOLATE_REQ_REG), the two DFT abort status pins, the subsystem reset-complete vector, the primary-chiplet strap, and the FLR isolation path that raises both `isolate_req_o` and `skip_mem_repair_o`.
* Every leg compares an SMC CSR read, the boundary output or the SMC CPU interrupt vector against the contract, never against the value the bench drove; the FLR latch is required to hold when the pin drops and to release only on a software write.
* The NDM lane count comes from NDM_RESET.NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT. The programmer's guide states that this harness reads 4. The RDL bounds the field at 32 clusters and says to mask the request register with the count. The NDM compares use the value the register reports.
* The PLIC sits on the CPU cluster's local path (`fabric.adoc`) and is not reachable from the bench's JTAG2AXI manager; the external-interrupt leg observes the `cpu_interrupts_o` bits the SMC CPU interrupt vector map assigns to `smc_ext_interrupts_i` and does not claim PLIC pending state.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read NDM_RESET.NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT and derive the lane mask from it; drive `smc_ndmreset_request_i` through two alternating patterns within that mask and back to zero and read NDM_RESET.NDMRESET_REQUEST after each; write NDMRESET_PROCESS including all-ones and read `smc_ndmreset_process_o`.
. Drive `smc_ext_interrupts_i` lane 0, the top driven lane and an alternating pattern, sample `cpu_interrupts_o[31:0]` after each, drop the pins and sample it clear.
. Write and clear RESET_UNIT.SS_CONFIG, SYNC_REG and ISOLATE_REQ_REG and read `ss_config_o`, `sync_irq_o` and `isolate_req_o` after each.
. Pulse `mem_repair_abort_i` and `mbist_abort_i` and read DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU after release.
. Drive `ss_reset_complete_i` through three patterns and back to all-ones, reading RESET_UNIT.SS_RESET_COMPLETE after each; take `chiplet_is_primary_i` low and back, reading CPU_CTRL.SMC_ATTRIBUTES after each.
. Raise `cfg_flr_pf_active_i`, read ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG, `isolate_req_o` and `skip_mem_repair_o`, drop the pin and require the latch to hold; release it by software write.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-NDMRESET-REQ` |NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT reads within the 1..32 clusters `ndm_reset.rdl` allows and NDM_RESET.NDMRESET_REQUEST mirrors smc_ndmreset_request_i over that many lanes for two patterns and follows it back to zero
|`CHK-SMU-NDMRESET-PROC` |smc_ndmreset_process_o carries NDMRESET_PROCESS[NDMRESET_CLUSTER_COUNT-1:0], including the all-ones write that pins the port width to the reported count
|`CHK-SMU-EXT-IRQ` |smc_ext_interrupts_i lanes appear bit for bit on cpu_interrupts_o[31:0], the external-interrupt slice of the SMC CPU interrupt vector map (lane 0 is the bit the map assigns to PLIC source 1), and the level inputs release with the pins; PLIC pending state is not observed
|`CHK-SMU-SS-CONFIG` |ss_config_o carries RESET_UNIT.SS_CONFIG with SS_CONFIG_LOCK open, and clears with it
|`CHK-SMU-SYNC-IRQ` |sync_irq_o is RESET_UNIT.SYNC_REG.sync, set and cleared
|`CHK-SMU-ISOLATE-REQ` |isolate_req_o carries the ISOLATE_REQ_REG software term
|`CHK-SMU-DFT-ABORT` |mem_repair_abort_i and mbist_abort_i reach DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU and the fields hold after both pins are released
|`CHK-SMU-FLR-ISOLATE` |a cfg_flr_pf_active_i rising edge sets ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG, which raises isolate_req_o through ISOLATE_REQ_SMCEN_REG and skip_mem_repair_o; the latch holds when the pin drops and only a software write releases both
|`CHK-SMU-SS-RESET-COMPLETE` |RESET_UNIT.SS_RESET_COMPLETE mirrors ss_reset_complete_i lane for lane over three patterns and returns to all-ones with the pins released
|`CHK-SMU-CHIPLET-STRAP` |CPU_CTRL.SMC_ATTRIBUTES.chiplet_is_primary follows chiplet_is_primary_i low and back to set; the strap's OCTS system-timer role is not claimed
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-042-smu_telemetry_atb_capture_test]]
===== 42 `smu_telemetry_atb_capture_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_telemetry_atb_capture_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive of the wrapper telemetry ATB pins, one lane per receiver; `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI) to read the receiver registers; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: One complete ATB telemetry message entered at the wrapper pins is captured by each receiver and read back, the ATB flush handshake completes at the pins, and the ATB port applies backpressure when its receiver stops.

*Test Coverage*:

* Receiver 0 is read empty with PROBE_ID, COUNTER_VLDS and TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0] clear before any beat is driven (`hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/memmap.adoc`, "Reset Behavior"; field resets from the generated `telemetry_receiver.h`), so the capture leg starts from a known state and the later non-zero reads are transitions the reads themselves witnessed.
* With the telemetry clock gate proved open, a complete last-flagged message whose every beat handshook on `telemetry_atready_o` leaves the buffer non-empty, reports the framed probe id, one valid bit for the one counter's worth of valid blocks sent and that counter reassembled from the byte every valid block carried, and CTRL.BUFFER_POP returns it to empty.
* The frame is a DV-owned table in `seq_lib/smu_telemetry_atb_capture_seq.py` transcribed from the "Message Format" table of `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/index.adoc` (eight little-endian beats per 64-bit packet, `last_packet` at bit 63, `probe_id` at bits [60:56] of the first packet, seven 9-bit valid-plus-data blocks per packet, a counter as four consecutive blocks valid only when all four are valid), `doc/interface.adoc` (ATB_DATA_WIDTH 8, PACKET_WIDTH 64) and the generated `regs/gen/c/telemetry_receiver.h` (PROBE_ID width, COUNTER_VLDS bit `i` is counter `i`). The probe-id and last-packet compares therefore test the receiver against the documented format, not against itself.
* Specification gap: the document does not state which of the seven block positions is block 0, whether a block's valid bit is its top or bottom bit, or which block begins counter 0. The frame is built independent of all three (header block invalid under either orientation, every other block all ones), so what is proven for the counter is the round trip of a DV-chosen byte through COUNTER_VLDS and TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0]; most-significant-byte-first order and block placement are not discriminated until the specification fixes them.
* S5 to S7 repeat the capture on every receiver, each on its own lane with every ATB ID bit raised and then lowered, repeat the flush handshake on receivers 1 and 2, and run `clk_telemetry_i` (10 ns) faster than the receiver's `clk_smu_i` (12 ns) so that back-to-back beats outrun the ATB crossing FIFO and `telemetry_atready_o` falls after at least one accepted beat (`doc/interface.adoc`, "Back-pressure"); the number of beats accepted before the fall is recorded, since no document gives the crossing FIFO depth (spec gap). A complete message is queued before the beats, and CTRL.TELEMETRY_RX_FLUSH "discards queued messages and resets the assembly write pointer" (`telemetry_receiver.rdl`), so after the flush the buffer reads empty and a complete message sent afterwards is captured with its own probe id.
* CTRL.TELEMETRY_TX_FLUSH raises `telemetry_afvalid_o`; the request holds while `telemetry_afready_i` is low and retires when it is raised, at the pin and in the register field: `telemetry_afvalid_o` reads 1 after the register read that shows the field held, in the same step as `telemetry_afready_i` is raised, and 0 after it, so the pin's fall follows the acknowledgment. Lowering `telemetry_afready_i` again, read back at the wrapper port, leaves the request retired, so every receiver's acknowledgment both rises and falls at the pin. The expected behaviour is the `TELEMETRY_TX_FLUSH` field description in `regs/telemetry_receiver.rdl` (self-clearing on flush completion over the ATB AF interface) with `afvalid_o` / `afready_i` as request and acknowledgment in `doc/interface.adoc`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read receiver 0 STATUS, PROBE_ID, COUNTER_VLDS and TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0] at reset.
. Prove the telemetry clock gate open, drive the message framed per the DV-owned table beat by beat on the wrapper ATB pins, requiring `telemetry_atready_o` on each beat.
. Read the receiver registers, compare the probe id, the valid bits and counter 0 against the framed message, pop the buffer and read it empty again.
. Set CTRL.TELEMETRY_TX_FLUSH, hold `telemetry_afready_i` low and read the request held; sample `telemetry_afvalid_o` high, raise `telemetry_afready_i` and read the request retired; lower it and read it low at the wrapper port with the request still retired.
. For each receiver, drive a message framed with its own probe id on its own ATB lane with every ATB ID bit high, return the lane to zero data and ID, read the probe id, pop the buffer and read it empty.
. Repeat the flush handshake on receivers 1 and 2.
. With `clk_telemetry_i` running faster than `clk_smu_i`, on each receiver's lane in turn send a complete message and read the buffer non-empty, hold `telemetry_atvalid_i` high until `telemetry_atready_o` falls while counting accepted beats, drop it and wait for ready to rise; pulse CTRL.TELEMETRY_RX_FLUSH and read the buffer empty; send a complete message again, read its probe id and pop it.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-TEL-RESET` |receiver 0 reads STATUS.BUFFER_EMPTY set with PROBE_ID, COUNTER_VLDS and TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0] clear before any beat is driven
|`CHK-SMU-TEL-CAPTURE` |a complete last-flagged ATB message framed per the documented format, whose every beat handshook on telemetry_atready_o, leaves the buffer non-empty, reports the framed probe id, one valid bit for the one counter's worth of valid blocks sent and counter 0 equal to the byte that filled them, and CTRL.BUFFER_POP returns it to empty
|`CHK-SMU-TEL-FLUSH` |CTRL.TELEMETRY_TX_FLUSH raises telemetry_afvalid_o and the request holds while telemetry_afready_i is low, with the pin still 1 when telemetry_afready_i rises; raising it retires the request at the pin and clears the register field, and lowering it again at the wrapper port leaves the request retired, on every receiver
|`CHK-SMU-TEL-EVERY-RECEIVER` |each receiver takes a message on its own ATB lane, reports the probe id framed for it, and pops back to empty
|`CHK-SMU-TEL-BACKPRESSURE` |on every receiver, with a message queued, back-to-back beats on a telemetry clock faster than the receiver clock make `telemetry_atready_o` fall after at least one accepted beat, it rises again once the beats stop, the RX flush empties the buffer, and a message sent after the flush reads back its own probe id
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-043-smu_smc_peripheral_irq_test]]
===== 43 `smu_smc_peripheral_irq_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_peripheral_irq_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the GPIO and UART registers); TB pad drive; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The raw GPIO and UART interrupt vectors `port_table.adoc` brings out of the wrapper follow their sources both ways.

*Test Coverage*:

* GPIO pin 0 is taken from its LSIO owner through its SMC register so the pad input buffer opens, armed as an active-high level, and the pad is driven: `gpio_interrupt_o[0]` and DATA_CTRL.PAD2CORE must follow it up and down with no other lane moving.
* With the UART clock gate read open, enabling IER.ETBEI must raise `uart_interrupt_o[0]` alone, IIR must report pending with the INTERRUPT_ID code `uart_16550_main.rdl` assigns to Transmitter Holding Register Empty, and the IIR read must retire it.
* Golden sources: GPIO DATA_CTRL field masks, the ENABLE_RX_TX "RX enabled" encoding and INTERRUPT_TYPE's active-high-level reset encoding from `hw/ip/gpio/regs/gpio_intf.rdl` through its PeakRDL header; UART register offsets and IER / IIR field masks from the PeakRDL headers of `hw/ip/uart/uart_16550/regs/uart_16550_main.rdl`; the transmitter-empty identification code parsed from that RDL's IIR.INTERRUPT_ID field description (`0x1`, the 16550 encoding); the UART clock-gate mask from `smc_base_config.h`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the GPIO pin 0 mux and interrupt configuration over JTAG2AXI; drive the pad high and low and read `gpio_interrupt_o` and DATA_CTRL.PAD2CORE after each.
. Read the UART clock gate open, set IER.ETBEI, read `uart_interrupt_o` and IIR, then read IIR again and require the interrupt retired.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-GPIO-IRQ` |GPIO pin 0 taken from its LSIO owner and armed as an active-high level: driving the pad raises gpio_interrupt_o[0] and DATA_CTRL.PAD2CORE with no other lane moving, and both release with the pad
|`CHK-SMU-UART-IRQ` |with the UART clock gate read open, enabling IER.ETBEI raises uart_interrupt_o[0] alone, IIR reports pending with the INTERRUPT_ID code the UART RDL assigns to Transmitter Holding Register Empty, and the IIR read retires it
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-138-smu_dtp_debug_control_fields_test]]
===== 138 `smu_dtp_debug_control_fields_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_dtp_debug_control_fields_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_dtp_debug_control_fields_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP (DEBUG_CONTROL TDR scans, TRST through `assert_trst` and `release_trst`, five TMS highs for Test-Logic-Reset); bench pad inputs `xtrig_ctm_dst_req`, `xtrig_ctm_src_ack`, `xtrig_clk_stop_req` (driven as the lane request), `tb_xtrig_ctp_req_in_din` and `tb_xtrig_ctp_ack_in_din`, which the leaf requires idle at start; `dtp_stop_clks_o` and `jtag_ptap_state` sampled on `clk_smu_i`; `powergood_i` and `obs_powergood_stable_o` on `clk_ref_i` for the power-on reset leg; the primary reset `rst_primary_smc_clk_no`; DEBUG_CONTROL bits 0 (boot_stall), 1 (boot_stall_ovrd), 2 (cla_clock_stop_en), 3 (jtag_clock_stop) and 4 (cla_clock_stop); the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: the DEBUG_CONTROL TDR of the primary TAP resets to zero, stores bits 0 to 3, reports bit 4 from the clock-stop request lane alone, drives `dtp_stop_clks_o` from `jtag_clock_stop` within the leaf's bound, and clears `jtag_clock_stop` and the stop output on TRST, Test-Logic-Reset and power-on reset.

*Test Coverage*:

* With every cross-trigger and CTP pad input idle, DEBUG_CONTROL reads 0 in bits 3:0 and in bit 4 after reset; `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc` ("Debug Control") gives reset 0 for all five bits.
* Each of bits 0 to 3, written alone with 1 and then with 0, reads back as written with the other bits 0; the document marks them R/W.
* With no request, bit 4 reads 0 in the capture of the scan that shifts 1 into it and 0 on the read that follows that scan; it reads 1 with `xtrig_clk_stop_req` raised while 0 is shifted in, and 0 again after the request drops; the document marks bit 4 read-only and says the TDR ignores data shifted into read-only fields, and `hw/sys/dtp/doc/clk_rst.adoc` ("Clock Stop") says it reports the CLA clock-stop request.
* Writing `jtag_clock_stop` makes `dtp_stop_clks_o` rise, and clearing it makes the output fall, each within a bound of 64 `clk_smu_i` edges counted from the Update-DR of the write scan; the bound is the value the leaf sets, and the measured edge counts are logged. `clk_rst.adoc` ("Signal Flow") gives three `clk_i` cycles from the request to `stop_clks_o`, and the leaf does not compare that count.
* After each of TRST, Test-Logic-Reset through five TMS highs and a power-on reset by `powergood_i`, with `jtag_clock_stop` and `cla_clock_stop_en` set and `dtp_stop_clks_o` observed at 1, `jtag_clock_stop` reads 0 and `dtp_stop_clks_o` is 0 both after the reset and after the read; `clk_rst.adoc` states that `jtag_clock_stop` resets on all three. The value of `cla_clock_stop_en` after each source is read and logged and is not compared, because no sentence in those documents says which reset sources restore it.
* The effect of the CLA clock-stop request on `dtp_stop_clks_o` and the SMC CLA path that drives lane 0 are out of scope: the leaf drives the lane request only to observe bit 4.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for reset done, require the cross-trigger and CTP pad inputs idle, reset the TAP, wait for the primary reset to release, wait a fixed 16 `clk_smu_i` cycles, and require `dtp_stop_clks_o` low.
. Read DEBUG_CONTROL and compare bits 3:0 and bit 4 with 0.
. For each of bits 0 to 3, write the bit alone, read it back, write 0 and read it back.
. Read DEBUG_CONTROL with bit 4 shifted high, read it plainly, raise `xtrig_clk_stop_req` and read with 0 shifted in, lower it and read again.
. Write `jtag_clock_stop` high and count `clk_smu_i` edges from Update-DR until `dtp_stop_clks_o` rises, then write 0 and count until it falls.
. For TRST, Test-Logic-Reset and power-on reset in turn, set `jtag_clock_stop` and `cla_clock_stop_en`, wait for `dtp_stop_clks_o` high, read back the written value, apply the reset, wait a fixed 16 `clk_smu_i` cycles, then sample `dtp_stop_clks_o`, read DEBUG_CONTROL and sample the output again.
. Log the last state of every bounded wait, require none expired, clear DEBUG_CONTROL, and require the steps ran in order.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DBGCTL-RESET-VALUES` |DEBUG_CONTROL bits 3:0 and bit 4 read 0 after reset with every request idle
|`CHK-DBGCTL-RW-BITS` |each of bits 0 to 3 reads back the written 1 and the written 0 with the other bits 0
|`CHK-DBGCTL-BIT4-READONLY` |bit 4 reads 0 on the read that follows a scan shifting 1 into it with no request, and 1 with a lane request while 0 is shifted in
|`CHK-JTAG-STOP-LATENCY` |`dtp_stop_clks_o` rises and falls within 64 `clk_smu_i` edges of the Update-DR of the `jtag_clock_stop` write
|`CHK-DBGCTL-RESET-CLEARS` |TRST, Test-Logic-Reset and power-on reset each leave `jtag_clock_stop` at 0 and `dtp_stop_clks_o` at 0, after the set value was read back
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |none of the bounded stop-output, reset-done, power-on and Update-DR waits expires
|`CHK-NONVAC` |the idle, reset-value, storage, read-only, latency, reset-clearing and wait-report steps complete in order with a positive step time
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-139-smu_octs_reset_and_secondary_hold_test]]
===== 139 `smu_octs_reset_and_secondary_hold_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_octs_reset_and_secondary_hold_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_octs_reset_and_secondary_hold_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP and the SMC JTAG2AXI bridge (with `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read as open); the bench probe `tb_timer_count`, which carries `timer_count_o`, sampled on `clk_smu_i`; the bench strap `tb_chiplet_secondary`, which the leaf drives high to make `chiplet_is_primary_i` low; the OCTS registers CTRL, TIMER_START, TIMER_PRESET_LO, TIMER_PRESET_HI, TIMER_COUNT_LO, TIMER_COUNT_HI, CREDIT_EXPIRED, TIMER_GPIO_ENABLE and STATUS; the SMC CPU control register SMC_ATTRIBUTES; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: the system timer OCTS reads its documented reset state with `timer_count_o` at zero from the bench's reset-done indication, and with the chiplet strap low it reports SECONDARY mode and ignores a TIMER_START write, while at the default strap it reports PRIMARY mode and a TIMER_START write runs the timer.

*Test Coverage*:

* CTRL reads the word its three reset fields give in `hw/ip/system_timer_octs/regs/system_timer_octs.rdl` (STEP 0x1, PULSE_WIDTH 0x2, CREDIT_VAL 0xA, so `0x0001_020A`); TIMER_START, TIMER_PRESET_LO, TIMER_PRESET_HI, TIMER_COUNT_LO, TIMER_COUNT_HI, CREDIT_EXPIRED and TIMER_GPIO_ENABLE read 0; STATUS.RUNNING reads 0.
* `timer_count_o` is 0 on every `clk_smu_i` sample taken from the point the bench reports reset done, not from reset release, until the leaf writes TIMER_START; `hw/ip/system_timer_octs/doc/interface.adoc` ("Timer Count") describes `timer_count_o` as the live count, and the RDL gives the count registers a reset of 0.
* With the strap driven so that `chiplet_is_primary_i` is low, SMC_ATTRIBUTES.chiplet_is_primary reads 0 (`hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/cpu_ctrl/cpu_ctrl.rdl`, driven by strap; `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`, `chiplet_is_primary_i`) and STATUS.MODE reads 1, SECONDARY (`system_timer_octs.rdl`). The first read after the strap change is logged and never compared; the compared reads follow 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles later. The control for this leg is the pair read at the default strap before it is driven: SMC_ATTRIBUTES.chiplet_is_primary reads 1 and STATUS.MODE reads 0, PRIMARY.
* In SECONDARY mode a write of 1 to TIMER_START has no effect (`system_timer_octs.rdl`, TIMER_START): STATUS.RUNNING reads 0 afterwards and `timer_count_o` is 0 on each of 1024 consecutive `clk_smu_i` samples, with the preset the leaf programs written first.
* The control for the SECONDARY hold: after the strap is restored to its primary default and 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles pass, a write of 1 to TIMER_START sets STATUS.RUNNING, and `timer_count_o` changes within 1024 `clk_smu_i` samples (`hw/ip/system_timer_octs/doc/interface.adoc`: a PRIMARY advances the count by one each `clk_i` cycle). The timer has no stop control (`hw/ip/system_timer_octs/doc/architecture.adoc`), so it is left running at the end of the leaf.
* The sync-load and credit pulses and the count values are out of scope; the PRIMARY start is checked only for STATUS.RUNNING and a changing `timer_count_o`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for reset done within its bound, start the `timer_count_o` sampler, reset the TAP, read the IDCODE and require the JTAG2AXI gate open.
. Read CTRL, the seven zero-reset registers, STATUS and SMC_ATTRIBUTES over JTAG2AXI, compare each CSR with its reset value, and compare `chiplet_is_primary` with 1 and STATUS.MODE with PRIMARY.
. Compare the sampled `timer_count_o` values since reset done with 0.
. Drive `tb_chiplet_secondary` high, wait 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles, read SMC_ATTRIBUTES and STATUS and log them, wait 64 more cycles, and read both again and compare `chiplet_is_primary` with 0 and STATUS.MODE with SECONDARY.
. Write TIMER_PRESET_LO and TIMER_PRESET_HI, stop the sampler, write 1 to TIMER_START, read STATUS.RUNNING, and sample `timer_count_o` on 1024 consecutive `clk_smu_i` edges, then restore `tb_chiplet_secondary` to 0.
. Wait 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles, write 1 to TIMER_START, read STATUS.RUNNING, wait up to 1024 `clk_smu_i` edges for `timer_count_o` to change, and require RUNNING 1 and a changed count.
. Log the last state of every bounded wait and sampler and require none expired.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OCTSR-RESET-CSR` |CTRL reads its reset word, the seven other CSRs read 0 and STATUS.RUNNING reads 0
|`CHK-OCTSR-RESET-PIN` |`timer_count_o` is 0 on every `clk_smu_i` sample from the bench's reset-done indication, not reset release, until the TIMER_START write
|`CHK-OCTSR-STRAP-LOW` |at the default strap SMC_ATTRIBUTES.chiplet_is_primary reads 1 and STATUS.MODE reads PRIMARY, and with the strap low they read 0 and SECONDARY
|`CHK-OCTSR-SECONDARY-HOLD` |in SECONDARY mode TIMER_START is ignored: STATUS.RUNNING reads 0 and `timer_count_o` is 0 for 1024 consecutive `clk_smu_i` samples; with the strap restored, a TIMER_START write in PRIMARY mode sets STATUS.RUNNING and `timer_count_o` changes within 1024 samples
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |none of the bounded JTAG2AXI, reset-done, pin-sampler and run-count waits expires
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-140-smu_sep_mailbox_sba_readback_test]]
===== 140 `smu_sep_mailbox_sba_readback_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_mailbox_sba_readback_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_sep_mailbox_sba_readback_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`, which extends the SEP system-bus sweep sequence and runs the bring-up of the `smu_dtp_sep_dm_dmi_test` sequence first)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` on the primary TAP with the SEP STAP selected through TAP_3DCR and the SEP debug module's DMI registers (dmcontrol, dmstatus, sbcs, sbaddress0, sbdata0) used as the system bus access path; the bench taps `tb_stap_sep_tms` and `tb_stap_sep_tck`; `smc_lc_state_in_o` and `lcc_dbg_disable_o` sampled on `clk_smu_i`; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_test_dev.preload`, the TEST_DEV eFuse shadow image; the sixteen SEP mailbox ports (`outbound_mailbox_0` to `outbound_mailbox_7` and `inbound_mailbox_0` to `inbound_mailbox_7`) at the addresses of the generated SEP address header; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: WIRQT of each of the sixteen SEP mailbox ports reads 0 and then reads back the threshold written to it through the SEP debug module's system bus, with every bus access completing without a system-bus error.

*Test Coverage*:

* Under the TEST_DEV lifecycle image the exported lifecycle state leaves its pre-sense value and `lcc_dbg_disable_o` reads 0, so the SEP debug path is open; the leaf requires both before it selects the SEP STAP, and the debug module answers a dmstatus read with status 0 and version 2 (RISC-V Debug Specification 0.13) after dmactive is set.
* WIRQT at offset `0x20` of each port reads 0 before any write, as `hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/regs/axil_mailbox.rdl` gives for the field's reset.
* For mailbox m from 0 to 7, the value `1 + (m % 7)` is written to WIRQT of the outbound port and of the inbound port and each reads back unchanged; the value is the one the leaf programs, chosen below the FIFO depth of eight entries that `hw/sys/sep/doc/mailbox.adoc` gives, since the RDL reduces a written value of the depth or more to depth minus 1.
* The mailbox layout (mailbox m at 4 kB steps, outbound port at offset `0x000` and inbound port at `0x800`) is stated in `mailbox.adoc`; the leaf takes the port addresses from the generated SEP header.
* Every system-bus read returns sberror 0, and every WIRQT is written back to 0 after the compare; the write responses of those restoring writes are required to carry no error.
* The interrupt a WIRQT threshold raises, the FIFO behaviour and the SMC-side view of the mailboxes are out of scope here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Require the TEST_DEV preload plusarg, wait for the exported lifecycle state to leave its pre-sense value, check the debug disable posture, reset the TAP and read the IDCODE.
. Select the SEP STAP through TAP_3DCR, read the PTAP IDCODE through the spliced network, load the dmi instruction, set dmactive and read dmstatus.
. For each mailbox from 0 to 7 and for its outbound and inbound port, read WIRQT, write the threshold, and read it back through the system bus.
. Write 0 back to WIRQT of all sixteen ports.
. Log the last state of the system-bus busy poll and the DMI busy retry and require none expired.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-COMP-SEP-WIRQT-READBACK` |WIRQT of all sixteen SEP mailbox ports reads 0, then reads back the threshold below the FIFO depth written over the debug system bus, with sberror 0
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |neither the system-bus busy poll nor the DMI busy retry expires
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-141-smu_smc_aperture_reset_state_test]]
===== 141 `smu_smc_aperture_reset_state_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_aperture_reset_state_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_aperture_reset_state_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP and the SMC JTAG2AXI bridge (with `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read as open); the `smc_region_size_o` output sampled on `clk_smu_i`; the SMC base configuration registers GLOBAL_BASE, LOCAL_BASE, REGION_SIZE and CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL at the addresses and field masks of the generated SMC headers; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: after reset the SMC base configuration reports its documented aperture bases and region size, `smc_region_size_o` carries the region size, and the five peripheral clock-gate enables read 0 with the hysteresis at its reset value.

*Test Coverage*:

* GLOBAL_BASE reads `0x4000_0000`, LOCAL_BASE reads `0xC000_0000` and the REGION_SIZE size field reads `0x0100_0000`, the field resets of `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/smc_base_config/smc_base_config.rdl`, read as 64-bit JTAG2AXI accesses with nothing written first.
* `smc_region_size_o` equals the REGION_SIZE reset value on the first `clk_smu_i` sample taken after the register reads; `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` lists the port as the SMC address region size output.
* CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL read as one 32-bit access shows `i3c_cg_en`, `avs_cg_en`, `i2c_cg_en`, `uart_cg_en` and `telemetry_cg_en` at 0 and `cg_hysteresis` at its reset value `0x1f`, as `smc_base_config.rdl` gives; the other enable bits of the register are not compared.
* The leaf reads only and writes no register, so write behaviour of the aperture registers, the effect of a programmed REGION_SIZE on `smc_region_size_o` and the effect of a clock-gate enable on its peripheral window are out of scope here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Reset the TAP, read the IDCODE and require the JTAG2AXI gate open.
. Read GLOBAL_BASE, LOCAL_BASE and REGION_SIZE as 64-bit accesses and compare each field with its reset value.
. Sample `smc_region_size_o` on a `clk_smu_i` edge and compare it with the REGION_SIZE reset value.
. Read CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL and compare the five peripheral enable bits and `cg_hysteresis` with their reset values.
. Log the last state of the JTAG2AXI wait and require it did not expire.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-APRT-BASES` |GLOBAL_BASE, LOCAL_BASE and REGION_SIZE read their RDL reset values over JTAG2AXI
|`CHK-APRT-REGION-PIN` |`smc_region_size_o` carries the REGION_SIZE reset value `0x0100_0000`
|`CHK-APRT-CLOCK-GATES` |the five peripheral `cg_en` bits read 0 and `cg_hysteresis` reads its reset value
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the bounded JTAG2AXI wait does not expire
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-142-smu_smc_scratch_bridge_readback_test]]
===== 142 `smu_smc_scratch_bridge_readback_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_scratch_bridge_readback_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_scratch_bridge_readback_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP and the SMC JTAG2AXI bridge (with `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read as open and `lc_state_o` sampled for the log); the sixteen SCRATCH registers of the SMC CPU control block at the addresses and stride of the generated SMC address header; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: each of the sixteen SMC CPU control SCRATCH registers reads 0 after reset and reads back a distinct value written to it over the JTAG2AXI bridge.

*Test Coverage*:

* SCRATCH[0] to SCRATCH[15] each read 0 before any write; `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/cpu_ctrl/cpu_ctrl.rdl` gives the `data[31:0]` field a reset of 0 and software read-write access, and places SCRATCH as sixteen registers at stride 8 from offset `0x80`.
* A 32-bit write of the pattern `(0xA5A50000 ^ (n * 0x01010101)) & 0xFFFFFFFF` to each SCRATCH[n] reads back unchanged over the same bridge; the pattern is the value the leaf programs, distinct for each n, so a readback at the wrong register is a mismatch.
* The leaf writes all sixteen registers before it reads any of them back, so a write that lands in a neighbouring register shows up as a wrong value.
* The CPU-side view of SCRATCH and the effect of a write on any other CPU control register are out of scope here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Reset the TAP, read the IDCODE, require the JTAG2AXI gate open and sample `lc_state_o` for the log.
. Read SCRATCH[0] to SCRATCH[15], write the pattern to each, and read each back.
. Compare every first read with 0 and every readback with its pattern.
. Log the last state of the JTAG2AXI wait and require it did not expire.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-COMP-SCRATCH-READBACK` |SCRATCH[0] to SCRATCH[15] read 0 at reset and read back the pattern written to each over JTAG2AXI
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the bounded JTAG2AXI wait does not expire
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-143-smu_ss_config_lane_drive_test]]
===== 143 `smu_ss_config_lane_drive_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_ss_config_lane_drive_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_ss_config_lane_drive_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP and the SMC JTAG2AXI bridge (with `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read as open); the bench probe `tb_ss_config`, which carries the 32-bit `ss_config_o` boundary output and is polled every four `clk_smu_i` cycles; the reset unit registers SS_CONFIG and SS_CONFIG_LOCK at the addresses of the generated SMC address header; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+expected_sep=1`, `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: each bit of the reset unit's SS_CONFIG register written over JTAG2AXI appears on its own lane of `ss_config_o` and on no other.

*Test Coverage*:

* SS_CONFIG_LOCK reads 0 before the first write, a precondition the leaf requires so that every SS_CONFIG bit is writable; `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/reset_unit/reset_unit.rdl` gives the lock reset 0 and says that a set lock bit stops the matching SS_CONFIG bit from being written.
* For i from 0 to 31, writing `1 << i` to SS_CONFIG makes `ss_config_o` equal `1 << i` within a bound of 64 polls of four `clk_smu_i` cycles; the polling bound is the value the leaf sets and the 32-bit `ss_config` field of the RDL and `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` (`ss_config_o [31:0]`, subsystem configuration output) give the one-to-one lane mapping.
* Writing `0xFFFF_FFFF` makes `ss_config_o` equal `0xFFFF_FFFF`, and writing 0 makes it equal 0, within the same bound.
* A read of SS_CONFIG after the zero write returns 0.
* The reset value of SS_CONFIG and the value of `ss_config_o` at reset are not compared; the RDL gives 0 and the leaf's first compared sample follows its first write.
* The use of each lane by a subsystem and the effect of a set SS_CONFIG_LOCK bit are out of scope here: the RDL says the use is integration-specific, and the leaf never writes SS_CONFIG_LOCK.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for reset done, reset the TAP, read the IDCODE, require the JTAG2AXI gate open and read SS_CONFIG_LOCK, requiring 0.
. For each lane from 0 to 31, write `1 << lane` to SS_CONFIG, poll `ss_config_o` until it equals the value or the bound expires, and compare it.
. Write all ones and compare `ss_config_o`, write 0 and compare `ss_config_o`, then read SS_CONFIG and compare it with 0.
. Log the last state of the JTAG2AXI and pin waits, require none expired and the wait counts to match the declared accesses, and require positive simulated time between the starts of steps 1 to 4.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-COMP-SS-CONFIG-SINGLE-LANE` |writing `1 << i` to SS_CONFIG leaves `ss_config_o` equal to `1 << i` for each of the 32 lanes
|`CHK-COMP-SS-CONFIG-ALL-ONES-ZERO` |writing all ones and then all zeros drives `ss_config_o` to the same value and SS_CONFIG then reads 0
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |none of the bounded JTAG2AXI and `ss_config_o` waits expires
|`CHK-NONVAC` |the setup, lane, and all-ones and zero steps each take positive simulated time before the wait-report step starts
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-144-smu_telemetry_queue_threshold_test]]
===== 144 `smu_telemetry_queue_threshold_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_telemetry_queue_threshold_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_telemetry_queue_threshold_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP and the SMC JTAG2AXI bridge (with `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read as open) for every receiver register access; the bench ATB lane 0 pins `tb_telemetry_atdata`, `tb_telemetry_atid` (driven with ATB ID `0x2A`), `tb_telemetry_atvalid` and `tb_telemetry_atready`, sampled on `clk_ref_i`; telemetry receiver 0 registers CTRL, STATUS, TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID, INTR_STATUS and INTR_TEST; SMC base configuration CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL for the telemetry clock gate; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+expected_sep=1`, `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: receiver 0 queues eight ATB messages, exposes the second to eighth in arrival order as the queue is popped, reports empty and full at the queue ends, and raises the buffer-threshold interrupt status while the number of queued messages is above the programmed threshold.

*Test Coverage*:

* With the telemetry clock gate read open, a CTRL.BUFFER_POP write on the empty queue leaves STATUS.BUFFER_EMPTY set and TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID at 0; `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/architecture.adoc` ("Message Queue and Readout") says a pop advances the queue only when it is nonempty and an empty-queue read returns zero.
* With CTRL.BUFFER_THRESHOLD at 3, eight messages are sent one at a time, each as eight ATB beats forming one last-flagged packet whose probe id, 1 to 8 in turn, the leaf places in bits [60:56] and whose block layout is not observed (`hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/index.adoc`, "Message Format"). After each message STATUS.BUFFER_EMPTY reads 0, and STATUS.BUFFER_FULL reads 0 for the first seven messages and 1 after the eighth, the default queue depth of eight in `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/index.adoc` and `interface.adoc`.
* After each message INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD reads 0 up to three queued messages and 1 from the fourth, since the status is live while the occupancy is strictly greater than the threshold (`architecture.adoc`, "Interrupts"; `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/regs/telemetry_receiver.rdl`).
* A write of 1 to INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD with eight queued leaves it at 1, as the document says writing the status bit does not clear the source.
* Three successive pops expose probe ids 2, 3 and 4, then, with the threshold raised to 5 and five queued, INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD reads 0; with the threshold set back to 3 it reads 1; two more pops expose 5 and 6 with the status reading 1 at four queued and 0 at three; the last three pops expose 7, 8 and then 0 on the emptied queue.
* On the empty queue, writing 1 to INTR_TEST.BUFFER_THRESHOLD makes INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD read 1 and writing 0 releases it to 0, as the RDL gives.
* The probe id of the first message, visible at the head before the first pop, is not read; arrival order is compared only for probe ids 2 to 8, as each pop exposes the next.
* INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD is read as a register only, so the interrupt output at the SMU boundary is out of scope. The behaviour of a ninth message arriving on a full queue is out of scope.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for reset done, reset the TAP, read the IDCODE, require the JTAG2AXI gate open and read CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL, requiring the telemetry clock gate enable clear.
. Write CTRL.BUFFER_POP on the empty queue, read STATUS and TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID.
. Write CTRL.BUFFER_THRESHOLD 3, then send eight messages with probe ids 1 to 8, reading STATUS and INTR_STATUS after each.
. Write 1 to INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD and read it, then pop three times reading TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID and INTR_STATUS after each pop.
. Write CTRL.BUFFER_THRESHOLD 5 and read INTR_STATUS, write 3 and read it, then pop twice and read TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID and INTR_STATUS after each, and pop three more times reading TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID.
. Write 1 and then 0 to INTR_TEST.BUFFER_THRESHOLD, reading INTR_STATUS after each, and restore CTRL and the ATB pins.
. Log the last state of the JTAG2AXI and ATB beat waits, require none expired, and require both wait sites to have run.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-TELQ-POP-EMPTY` |a pop on the empty queue leaves BUFFER_EMPTY set and PROBE_ID 0
|`CHK-TELQ-EMPTY-FULL` |BUFFER_EMPTY reads 0 after each message and BUFFER_FULL sets only on the eighth
|`CHK-TELQ-THRESHOLD-LIVE` |INTR_STATUS.BUFFER_THRESHOLD is 0 up to three queued messages and 1 above the threshold of 3, and stays 1 while seven to five messages remain and when the threshold returns to 3 with five queued
|`CHK-TELQ-ORDER` |successive pops expose probe ids 2 to 8 in arrival order, then 0 when empty
|`CHK-TELQ-THRESHOLD-CLEAR` |a write of 1 leaves the status at 1, raising the threshold to 5 with five queued reads 0, and draining from four to three queued reads 1 then 0
|`CHK-TELQ-THRESHOLD-TEST` |INTR_TEST.BUFFER_THRESHOLD forces the status to 1 and releases it to 0
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |none of the bounded JTAG2AXI and ATB beat waits expires
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''

[[sec-145-smu_telemetry_reset_state_test]]
===== 145 `smu_telemetry_reset_state_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_telemetry_reset_state_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_telemetry_reset_state_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` primary TAP and the SMC JTAG2AXI bridge (with `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` read as open); the registers CTRL, STATUS, INTR_STATUS, INTR_ENABLE, INTR_TEST, TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID, TELEMETRY_COUNTER_VLDS and TELEMETRY_COUNTER of the three telemetry receivers at the addresses of the generated SMC address header; SMC base configuration CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL; the run mode `sep_rtl` plusargs `+expected_sep=1`, `+rom_hex=smc_rom_default.hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex=smc_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex=sep_efuse_default.hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex=sep_boot_rom_default.hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex=sep_itcm_default.hex`, `+sep_dtcm_hex=sep_dtcm_default.hex`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+smc_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/default_efuse_shadow_reg.preload` and `+sep_shadow_reg_preload=hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/default_sep_efuse_shadow_reg.preload`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: before any ATB beat is driven, the three telemetry receivers read their documented reset state, with an empty queue and zero control, interrupt and message registers.

*Test Coverage*:

* On each of the three receivers STATUS reads BUFFER_EMPTY set and BUFFER_FULL clear, the field resets of `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/regs/telemetry_receiver.rdl`; only the two defined status fields are compared.
* On each receiver CTRL, INTR_STATUS, INTR_ENABLE and INTR_TEST read 0, as the RDL gives and as `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/architecture.adoc` ("Reset and Flush") says reset clears the control and interrupt registers.
* On each receiver TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID and TELEMETRY_COUNTER_VLDS read 0, and TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0] reads 0, with TELEMETRY_COUNTER[1] to TELEMETRY_COUNTER[31] also read on receiver 0; the architecture document says an empty-queue read returns zero although queue payload storage is not reset.
* CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL is read and its value is logged and never compared, so a closed telemetry clock gate does not fail the leaf and the receiver registers are read as the bridge returns them.
* The leaf writes nothing and drives no ATB beat, so the behaviour of the registers after a message, a pop or a flush is out of scope here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for reset done, reset the TAP, read the IDCODE, require the JTAG2AXI gate open and read CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL for the log.
. For each receiver read STATUS, CTRL, INTR_STATUS, INTR_ENABLE and INTR_TEST and compare each with its reset value.
. For each receiver read TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID, TELEMETRY_COUNTER_VLDS and TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0], and for receiver 0 also TELEMETRY_COUNTER[1] to TELEMETRY_COUNTER[31], and compare each with 0.
. Log the last state of the JTAG2AXI wait and require it did not expire.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-TELR-CTRL-STATUS` |STATUS reads BUFFER_EMPTY set and BUFFER_FULL clear, and CTRL, INTR_STATUS, INTR_ENABLE and INTR_TEST read 0, on all three receivers
|`CHK-TELR-MESSAGE-ZERO` |TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID, TELEMETRY_COUNTER_VLDS and the counters read 0 on the empty queue
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the bounded JTAG2AXI wait does not expire
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
