*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-075-smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test]]
===== 75 `smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: SMC eFuse bank model inside the wrapper composition (declared in *Bench stand-ins and exceptions*); `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl_fuse_sense` run mode, which leaves `+skip_fuse_sense` unset

*Objective*: SMC fuse sense completes once after the primary reset released and after eFuse read traffic appeared, and the delayed fuse reset releases after that rise rather than with the cold reset.

*Test Coverage*:

* Measured against the `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` and `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` rows of `port_table.adoc`; the table names the two ports but not their relative order, so the ordering leg is a check on the elaborated design with no document behind it, recorded as such.
* eFuse traffic is recognised on the SMU eFuse shim command port by the `valid` bit of the command structs as `hw/ip/efuse/doc/interface.adoc` ("Fuse Command Protocol") lays them out.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm the edge and eFuse-command observers ahead of bring-up.
. After the SMC primary reset releases, require SMC eFuse read traffic and then exactly one rising edge of `smc_fuse_sense_done_o`, after both.
. Require `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` to release after that rise and not at the cold-reset release.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-FUSE-SENSE-S1` |smc_fuse_sense_done_o rises once, after reset release and after SMC eFuse traffic
|`CHK-SMU-FUSE-SENSE-S3` |smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o releases after that rise, not with the cold reset
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-076-smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test]]
===== 76 `smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: the checked-in SMC ROM stub `hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/min_pass.rom.hex` (no toolchain); `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI) for the external readback; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The SMC CPU fetches and executes the checked-in ROM stub on the wrapper and lands its magic word in CPU_CTRL scratch0, visible to an external master.

*Test Coverage*:

* The scratch0 read is the word the stub stores, so the fetch, the execution and the CSR write all landed; the SMC ROM read counter must have advanced across the boot, so the instruction stream came out of the ROM macro rather than a residual scratch image.
* An external master reading scratch0 at its architectural address over the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge sees the same magic, so the store landed in the CSR and not only in a testbench mirror.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage the ROM stub, bring the SMC up and wait for the scratch0 magic within the boot budget.
. Compare the ROM read counter before and after the boot.
. Read scratch0 over the fabric JTAG2AXI bridge and compare against the magic.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMC-ROM-BOOT-MAGIC` |SMC CPU_CTRL scratch0 reads the word the checked-in ROM stub stores, so the fetch, the execution and the CSR write all landed
|`CHK-SMC-ROM-BOOT-FETCH` |the SMC ROM read counter advanced across the boot, so the instruction stream came out of the ROM macro rather than a residual scratch image
|`CHK-SMC-ROM-BOOT-CSR-VISIBLE` |an external master reading SMC CPU_CTRL scratch0 at its architectural address over the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge sees the same magic, so the store landed in the CSR and not only in a testbench mirror
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-077-smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test]]
===== 77 `smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the watchdog and CPU_CTRL registers); `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The CORE0 watchdog's first and second timeouts reach the SMU pins, the watchdog timeout drops the SMC warm reset, and the warm reset clears the first timeout.

*Test Coverage*:

* <<sec-074-smu_smc_wdt_timeout_irq_test>> stops at the watchdog's pending bit because it never sets WDOGRSTEN, which "enables a sticky reset signal to outputted to top level" (`wdt.rdl`); this leaf sets it and follows the timeout out to `smc_wdt_first_timeout_o`, with WDOGIP0 set at the same time and both clear beforehand.
* The held first timeout runs the CPU_CTRL.WDT_TIMEOUT count (`cpu_ctrl.rdl`: "Cycle count value that 2nd stage watchdog timer timeout should occur at") out to `smc_wdt_second_timeout_o`. A watchdog timeout is a warm reset source (`hw/sys/smc/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, "Watchdog Timeout"; `hw/sys/smc/doc/cpu.adoc`: "a Warm Reset from a watchdog timeout"), so the SMC warm reset must drop; the cycles of the second timeout and of the drop are recorded.
* The warm reset covers the per-core watchdog timers (`clk_rst.adoc`, "Warm Reset"), so the first timeout must fall. The levels the second timeout, the SMC warm reset and the SMC watchdog reset settle at, and whether the watchdog reset asserted, are recorded.
* Spec gap: the SMC chapters do not state which watchdog stage asserts the warm reset, what the second timeout does after the warm reset, or whether the warm reset releases without the cold reset that `cpu.adoc` says follows a watchdog timeout; those are observations.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read both timeout pins and WDOGIP0 clear; arm CORE0 with WDOGRSTEN, WDOGENALWAYS and WDOGZEROCMP.
. Wait for `smc_wdt_first_timeout_o` and read WDOGIP0 set at the same time.
. Hold the first timeout, wait for `smc_wdt_second_timeout_o` and for the SMC warm reset to drop, recording both cycles.
. Wait for the first timeout to fall, and record the second timeout and both SMC resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-WDT-FIRST` |CORE0 armed with WDOGRSTEN as well as WDOGENALWAYS and WDOGZEROCMP raises smc_wdt_first_timeout_o, with WDOGIP0 set at the same time and both clear beforehand
|`CHK-SMU-WDT-SECOND` |the held first timeout runs the CPU_CTRL.WDT_TIMEOUT counter out to smc_wdt_second_timeout_o, and the SMC warm reset, released before, drops within the same window
|`CHK-SMU-WDT-RELEASE` |after the warm reset, smc_wdt_first_timeout_o falls
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-156-smu_smc_base_config_state_test]]
===== 156 `smu_smc_base_config_state_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_base_config_state_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_base_config_state_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, read-only accesses); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable`; `smc_region_size_o` sampled on `clk_smu_i`; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images; the register addresses and reset values the leaf compares come from the generated `smc_addr.h` and `smc_base_config.h` of the SMC register collateral

*Objective*: the SMC base-configuration block reads its documented reset values over JTAG2AXI after boot and `smc_region_size_o` equals the REGION_SIZE reset value.

*Test Coverage*:

* The block sits at `smc_base_config @ BASE_ADDR + 0x001_0000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`) and the register offsets and reset values are the SMC RDL's: GLOBAL_BASE at +0x0 resets to `0x4000_0000`, LOCAL_BASE at +0x8 to `0xC000_0000`, REGION_SIZE at +0x10 to size `0x100_0000` in bits [31:0], CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL at +0x18 to `cg_hysteresis` `0x1f` in bits [29:24] with every enable bit 0, the three HANG_DET control registers at +0x20, +0x30 and +0x40 to 0 and the three HANG_DET threshold registers at +0x28, +0x38 and +0x48 to `0x1000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/smc_base_config/smc_base_config.rdl`). The aperture reset values agree with `doc/integrator/src/smu-smc.adoc`, Address Map and Aperture.
* GLOBAL_BASE, LOCAL_BASE, REGION_SIZE and CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL are 64-bit registers read with 8-byte accesses; the REGION_SIZE read is masked to its size field before the compare. The HANG_DET registers declare no register width, so each is read with a 4-byte access and its 32-bit lane is compared.
* `smc_region_size_o` (`port_table.adoc`, "SMC address region size output") equals the REGION_SIZE reset value and the size read over JTAG2AXI.
* Every read must return JTAG2AXI status SUCCESS; nothing is written. The primary TAP IDCODE and the cleared `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate are bring-up preconditions the leaf requires before the first read, not checked values.
* The raw 64-bit reads, the hang-detector values and the port sample are logged as measured next to the compares.
* Writes, read-back after a write and the clock-gate behaviour are out of scope: the leaf proves reset state only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns and require the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate open.
. Read GLOBAL_BASE, LOCAL_BASE and REGION_SIZE with 8-byte accesses and compare them with their reset values.
. Sample `smc_region_size_o` on a `clk_smu_i` edge and compare it with the REGION_SIZE reset value and with the size read in step 2.
. Read CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL with an 8-byte access, then the three HANG_DET control and the three threshold registers with 4-byte accesses, and compare all seven with their reset values.
. Check that the reset-done wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finished inside their bounds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BOOT-BASECFG-APERTURE` |GLOBAL_BASE, LOCAL_BASE and the REGION_SIZE size field read `0x4000_0000`, `0xC000_0000` and `0x100_0000`
|`CHK-BOOT-BASECFG-REGION-PORT` |`smc_region_size_o` equals `0x100_0000` and the REGION_SIZE size read over JTAG2AXI
|`CHK-BOOT-BASECFG-CG-HANG` |CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL reads `0x1F00_0000`, the three hang-detector control registers read 0 and the three thresholds read `0x1000`
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the reset-done wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finish inside their bounds and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-157-smu_smc_cold_boot_release_order_test]]
===== 157 `smu_smc_cold_boot_release_order_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_cold_boot_release_order_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_cold_boot_release_order_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: stimulus pins `powergood_i`, `rst_cold_ni` and `jtag_trst` only, plus the 16-pulse TAP reset walk on `jtag_tck`; observation pins `rst_cold_stable_ref_clk_no` (`rst_cold_n_o` on the bench top), `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` (`rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o` on the bench top), `rst_primary_ref_clk_no` and the bench observation `obs_powergood_stable_o` (`u_dut.u_smu.powergood_stable`), sampled on every `clk_ref_i` edge, and `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` also on every `clk_smu_i` edge; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images

*Objective*: across two leaf-driven cold boots the stable power-good, the stable cold reset and the two primary resets hold, stretch and release as the SMC reset chapter states.

*Test Coverage*:

* Per `hw/sys/smc/doc/clk_rst.adoc` (Power-Good and Cold Reset), a 32-stage reference-clock synchronizer produces the stable power-good, cold reset assertion needs 32 consecutive low samples once released, and a 255-cycle reference-clock extension holds cold reset after the filtered input releases; the `rst_cold_ni` row of `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` says the same ("Deassertion is held for 255 cycles"). The primary reset is the AND of the stable cold reset and the cool-reset terms, so it cannot be released while the stable cold reset is asserted (`clk_rst.adoc`, Primary and Warm Reset): the `clk_ref_i` sampler compares that `rst_primary_ref_clk_no` reads 0 at every sample where the stable cold reset reads 0, and on the `clk_smu_i` sampler `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` reads released 28 samples before the exported stable cold reset in both boots, which is logged and not compared. Each primary reset is released synchronously to its destination clock by a four-stage synchronizer (`clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Synchronization).
* With `powergood_i` low and `rst_cold_ni` high for 600 `clk_ref_i` edges, the stable power-good and the stable cold reset read 0 on every sample, because power-good low holds the functional reset (`clk_rst.adoc`, reset sources table).
* After `powergood_i` rises, the stable power-good reads 0 for at least the first 32 `clk_ref_i` edges and rises exactly once in the first boot; the exact count is logged.
* The stable cold reset reads 0 for at least the first 255 `clk_ref_i` edges after the later of the `powergood_i` and `rst_cold_ni` rises; the exact count is logged.
* In the second boot `rst_cold_ni` is held low for 600 edges, released for 64 edges and driven low again for 64 edges; the stable cold reset reads 0 on every sample from its first 0 for as long as the input is low, stays 0 through the 64-edge release window, and after the final release rises within 5000 edges and no earlier than 255 edges after it. The edges before the stable cold reset first reads 0 after the input falls are logged and not compared, since the 32-cycle de-glitch is a clock-cycle condition and not a fixed delay.
* While the stable cold reset is asserted, `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` and `rst_primary_ref_clk_no` each read 0 on every sample of both boots' hold windows, and each reads 1 at the end of its boot after rising within its bounded wait; each has exactly one 0 -> 1 edge per boot and never reads 0 again after it.
* On every `clk_ref_i` sample of both boots, `rst_primary_ref_clk_no` reads 0 wherever the stable cold reset reads 0, since the primary reset is the AND of the stable cold reset and the cool-reset terms (`clk_rst.adoc`, Primary and Warm Reset).
* Measured and logged only: the order between the stable cold reset rise and each primary rise, the `clk_smu_i` samples and runs where `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` reads 1 while the stable cold reset reads 0, the assertion latency of each primary after the input falls, the latency in destination-clock edges from the stable cold release to each primary rise, and the count from the power-good rise to the stable power-good rise.
* The cool reset, the FLR cool reset, the watchdog reset terms and the warm reset are out of scope: the leaf drives none of them.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event, arm one sampler on `clk_smu_i` and one on `clk_ref_i`, drive `powergood_i`, `rst_cold_ni` and `jtag_trst` low and wait until the stable power-good, the stable cold reset and both primary resets read 0.
. Raise `rst_cold_ni` with `powergood_i` still low, hold 600 `clk_ref_i` edges and compare the stable power-good and stable cold reset samples with 0.
. Raise `powergood_i` and `jtag_trst`, start the TAP reset walk and count `clk_ref_i` edges until the stable power-good rises.
. Count the edges from the `powergood_i` rise, the later of the two input rises because `rst_cold_ni` is already high, to the stable cold reset rise and require at least 255.
. Wait for each primary reset rise of the first boot and compare the stable power-good edge count with 32 and its rise count with 1.
. Drive `rst_cold_ni` low for 600 edges, high for 64, low for 64 and high again, comparing the stable cold reset samples, then wait for its rise and require at least 255 edges after the final release.
. Wait for each primary reset rise of the second boot.
. Stop the samplers, compare both primary resets over the hold windows of both boots, compare `rst_primary_ref_clk_no` with 0 at every `clk_ref_i` sample where the stable cold reset reads 0, compare that each rose, is 1 at the end of its boot and has a single rising edge, and log the order and latency values.
. Check that every bounded wait finished inside its bound, then restore `powergood_i`, `rst_cold_ni` and `jtag_trst` high.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BOOT-PG-HOLD` |with `powergood_i` low and `rst_cold_ni` high for 600 `clk_ref_i` edges the stable power-good and the stable cold reset read 0 on every sample
|`CHK-BOOT-PG-STRETCH` |the stable power-good reads 0 for the first 32 `clk_ref_i` edges after `powergood_i` rises and rises exactly once
|`CHK-BOOT-COLD-HELD` |the stable cold reset reads 0 on every sample from its first 0 while `rst_cold_ni` is low, and stays 0 through the window after the first release request
|`CHK-BOOT-COLD-EXT` |the stable cold reset reads 0 for the first 255 `clk_ref_i` edges after the later of the `powergood_i` and `rst_cold_ni` rises
|`CHK-BOOT-COLD-REASSERT` |after a 64-edge `rst_cold_ni` pulse inside the extension the stable cold reset stays 0 from the first release request through the pulse and rises within the bound, no earlier than 255 edges after the final release
|`CHK-BOOT-PRIM-SMC-ORDER` |`rst_primary_smc_clk_no` reads 0 on every sample while the cold reset is asserted
|`CHK-BOOT-PRIM-REF-ORDER` |`rst_primary_ref_clk_no` reads 0 on every sample while the cold reset is asserted and at every `clk_ref_i` sample where the stable cold reset reads 0
|`CHK-BOOT-PRIM-EDGE` |each primary reset has exactly one 0 -> 1 edge per boot on its destination clock and does not read 0 afterwards
|`CHK-BOOT-COLD-TO-PRIMARY` |each primary reset rises within its bounded wait and reads 1 at the end of each boot
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |every bounded reset-pin wait and sampler of the leaf finishes inside its bound and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-158-smu_smc_cpu_ctrl_boot_state_test]]
===== 158 `smu_smc_cpu_ctrl_boot_state_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_cpu_ctrl_boot_state_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_cpu_ctrl_boot_state_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, read-only accesses); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` and the `smc_scratch_0_o` port; the `chiplet_is_primary_i` strap, which the leaf drives to primary by holding `tb_chiplet_secondary` at 0 with the idle inputs; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images; the register addresses and reset values the leaf compares come from the generated `smc_addr.h` and `cpu_ctrl.h` of the SMC register collateral

*Objective*: after boot the SMC CPU_CTRL registers read their documented reset values and the SMC_ATTRIBUTES fields decode the documented SMC configuration over JTAG2AXI.

*Test Coverage*:

* The block is `smc_cpu_ctrl @ BASE_ADDR + 0x003_9000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`) and every offset and reset value below is the RDL's (`hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/cpu_ctrl/cpu_ctrl.rdl`).
* RESET_VECTOR[0..3] at +0x0 each read `0xC004_0000`, the boot ROM base (`hw/sys/smc/doc/cpu.adoc`, reset vector paragraph). RESET_CTRL at +0x20 reads `0x10F` (the four core reset bits and the uncore reset bit at 1, every pulse-start bit and the debug-module reset bit at 0, so the debug module is held in reset as `cpu.adoc` states), CORE_RESET_PULSE_COUNT at +0x28 reads pre-reset count 8, post-reset count `0x10` and cores-done `0xF`, and RESET_TIMEOUT at +0x30 reads 0.
* WDT_TIMEOUT at +0x50 reads `0x4000` and WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET at +0x58 reads 0; SCRATCH[0..15] at +0x80 with an 8-byte stride read 0 in bits [31:0]. DUMMY_ROM_0..3 at +0x280 read `0x0145051300000517`, `0xFFF0069330551073`, `0x105000733046B073` and `0x00000000FFDFF06F`, and DUMMY_ROM_NULL[0..3] at +0x2A0 read 0.
* SMC_ATTRIBUTES at +0x200 decodes NUM_CORES 4 and SRAM_SIZE 20 (four cores and a 1 MiB scratchpad, `hw/sys/smc/doc/cpu.adoc`), NUM_MAILBOXES 32 and MAILBOX_DEPTH 2 (`hw/sys/smc/doc/periphs.adoc`, NumMailboxes and MailboxDepth rows of the SMC Peripheral Parameter Overrides table), NUM_EXT_INTERRUPTS 256 (`hw/sys/smc/doc/port_table.adoc`, `smc_ext_interrupts_i`), and CHIPLET_IS_PRIMARY 1: the field is "Driven by strap" (`cpu_ctrl.rdl`, SMC_ATTRIBUTES) and the strap value is the `chiplet_is_primary_i` level the leaf drives on the strap (`hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`, `chiplet_is_primary_i` row).
* WDT_TIMEOUT and WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET are read with 4-byte accesses and every other register with 8-byte accesses; every read must return JTAG2AXI status SUCCESS and nothing is written.
* Measured and logged only: bits [63:32] of every SCRATCH register, the `smc_scratch_0_o` value next to the SCRATCH[0] read, and the NO_OUTPUT_REMAP and NUM_CPU_INTERRUPTS fields of SMC_ATTRIBUTES; no document states the reset value of the SCRATCH upper word or the NO_OUTPUT_REMAP and NUM_CPU_INTERRUPTS values.
* Writes, the reset-control behaviour behind RESET_CTRL and the contents of the registers the leaf does not list are out of scope: the leaf proves reset state only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With `tb_chiplet_secondary` held at 0 from the idle inputs, wait for the reset-done event, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns and sample the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate.
. Read SCRATCH[0], log the `smc_scratch_0_o` value and the upper word, and compare bits [31:0] with the reset value.
. Read RESET_VECTOR[0..3] and compare each with the reset vector.
. Read RESET_CTRL, CORE_RESET_PULSE_COUNT and RESET_TIMEOUT and compare them with their reset values.
. Read WDT_TIMEOUT and WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET with 4-byte accesses and compare them with their reset values.
. Read SCRATCH[1..15], log the upper words and compare bits [31:0] with the reset value.
. Read SMC_ATTRIBUTES, log the two unlisted fields and compare the six listed fields.
. Read DUMMY_ROM_0..3 and DUMMY_ROM_NULL[0..3] and compare them with their words.
. Check that the reset-done wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finished inside their bounds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BOOT-SCRATCH0-RESET` |SCRATCH[0] bits [31:0] read 0 over JTAG2AXI after boot
|`CHK-BOOT-RESET-VECTORS` |RESET_VECTOR[0..3] each read `0xC004_0000`
|`CHK-BOOT-RESET-CTRL-VALUES` |RESET_CTRL, CORE_RESET_PULSE_COUNT and RESET_TIMEOUT read `0x10F`, `0xF_0010_0008` and 0
|`CHK-BOOT-WDT-TIMEOUT-RESET` |WDT_TIMEOUT reads `0x4000` and WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET reads 0
|`CHK-BOOT-SCRATCH-1-15` |SCRATCH[1..15] bits [31:0] each read 0 and the upper words are logged
|`CHK-BOOT-ATTRIBUTES` |SMC_ATTRIBUTES decodes 4 cores, SRAM_SIZE 20, 32 mailboxes, mailbox depth 2, 256 external interrupts and the primary strap at 1
|`CHK-BOOT-DUMMY-ROM` |DUMMY_ROM_0..3 read their four words and DUMMY_ROM_NULL[0..3] read 0
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the reset-done wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finish inside their bounds and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-159-smu_smc_efuse_shadow_map_readback_test]]
===== 159 `smu_smc_efuse_shadow_map_readback_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_efuse_shadow_map_readback_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_efuse_shadow_map_readback_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: the stream capture class `SmuEfuseStreamCapture` of the sequence, armed ahead of the shared bring-up, which records every rising `valid` of `smc_efuse_shim_command_resp` under `u_dut` (a 34-bit net, `{data[31:0], status, valid}`) and the rise of `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` on `clk_smu_i`; SMC eFuse bank model inside the wrapper composition (declared in *Bench stand-ins and exceptions*); `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, read-only accesses); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable`; the `smc_shadow_regs` port (`smc_shadow_regs_o` of `port_table.adoc`); `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl_fuse_sense` run mode, with `+smc_shadow_reg_preload` inert there because the preload plusargs are read only under `+skip_fuse_sense`; `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images, where `+smc_efuse_hex` names `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/smc_efuse_readback_pattern.hex` (one 32-bit word per line, word N on line N), which the sequence also reads as the expected words

*Objective*: the words the SMC eFuse sense streams across the shim are the words the SMC eFuse map returns over JTAG2AXI, both equal the eFuse image the run loads, and the eFuse interface reports the sense done.

*Test Coverage*:

* The sense stores each `valid` strobe's `data` into the shadow word at the running count, one 32-bit word per strobe, for a 256-word bank (`doc/integrator/src/smu-smc.adoc`, eFuse sensing paragraphs), and the SMC eFuse map exposes those shadow words from `smc_efuse_map @ BASE_ADDR + 0x000_7000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`; `hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/smc_efuse_map/smc_efuse_map.rdl`, address map). The response layout `{data, status, valid}` is `hw/ip/efuse/doc/interface.adoc`, Response Structure.
* The leaf requires the capture to hold exactly 256 strobes by the time `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` has risen and 64 further edges have passed.
* Sixteen map words at 4-byte offsets, word indices 0, 1, 2, 9, 10, 18, 27, 28, 29, 30, 31, 32, 64, 128, 200 and 255, each read with a 4-byte access over JTAG2AXI, and the stream words of the same indices equal the word of that index in the eFuse image the run loads; each map word also equals the stream word of the same index. The index set is the leaf's choice.
* The first word of each named map element equals its word in the eFuse image the run loads: LOCKS at +0x0, JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY at +0x8, I2C_I3C_ID[0] at +0x28, SMC_CONFIG at +0x70, OCCP_TRANSPORT_TIMEOUT at +0x78 and SPARE[0] at +0x80 (`smc_efuse_map.rdl`, register placement).
* EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS at `efuse_interface_ctrl @ BASE_ADDR + 0x000_8000` (`smc.rdl`) reads `efuse_sense_done` 1 after `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` has risen (`hw/ip/efuse/regs/efuse_interface_ctrl.rdl`, "Indicates if the eFuse state machine has completed").
* Measured and logged only: the first and last stream words, the number of strobes whose `status` bit is set, the per-index slices of `smc_shadow_regs` next to the stream words and whether each is equal, whether `smc_shadow_regs` is all zero, and the raw status word. The leaf does not read the status before the sense, assembles no multi-word element and does not compare `smc_shadow_regs`.
* The 240 map words the leaf does not read are out of scope.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm the stream capture before bring-up, read the `+smc_efuse_hex` image into 256 words, wait for the reset-done event and for the rise of `smc_fuse_sense_done_o`, require 256 captured strobes, log the stream, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns and require the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate open.
. Read the sixteen map words, compare each map word and the stream word of the same index with the image word of that index, and compare each map word with the stream word.
. Compare the first word of each of the six named map elements with its image word.
. Read EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS and require `efuse_sense_done` at 1.
. Sample `smc_shadow_regs` one `clk_smu_i` edge later, require it resolvable and log the per-index slices and whether it is all zero.
. Check that the capture, the done-rise wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finished inside their bounds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BOOT-SHADOW-SAMPLES` |the sixteen sampled SMC eFuse map words read over JTAG2AXI and the stream words of the same indices equal the words of the eFuse image the run loads, and the map words equal the stream words
|`CHK-BOOT-SHADOW-ELEMENTS` |the first word of each of the six named map elements equals its word in the eFuse image the run loads
|`CHK-BOOT-SENSE-STATUS` |`efuse_sense_done` of EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS reads 1 after `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` rose
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the stream capture, the done-rise wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finish inside their bounds and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-160-smu_smc_mailbox_aperture_layout_test]]
===== 160 `smu_smc_mailbox_aperture_layout_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_mailbox_aperture_layout_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_mailbox_aperture_layout_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, 4-byte reads and writes with all four byte strobes, plus one 8-byte read as a fallback); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable`; `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` (`rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o` on the bench top) and the bench net `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` (the `smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` port of `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`); `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images; the register addresses come from the generated `smc_addr.h` and the SMC_ATTRIBUTES field masks from the generated `cpu_ctrl.h` of the SMC register collateral

*Objective*: the SMC mailbox apertures answer each register at its documented offset, with per-aperture register state, and SMC_ATTRIBUTES reports 32 mailboxes of depth 2.

*Test Coverage*:

* The mailboxes sit at `smc_mailbox @ BASE_ADDR + 0x001_8000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`); outbound mailbox N begins at N * 0x1000 and the inbound aperture of pair N at N * 0x1000 + 0x800, and each aperture is its own mailbox instance sharing only the FIFO pair with its partner (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/regs/axil_mailbox_smc_wrap.rdl`; `hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/memmap.adoc`, Address Examples). The ten register offsets are WRITE_DATA +0x0, READ_DATA +0x8, STATUS +0x10, ERROR_FLAGS +0x18, WIRQT +0x20, RIRQT +0x28, IRQS +0x30, IRQEN +0x38, IRQP +0x40 and CTRL +0x48 (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/regs/axil_mailbox.rdl`, address map).
* Outbound mailbox 0, ten offsets: a read of WRITE_DATA returns `0xFEEDC0DE` with status SUCCESS, and a read of READ_DATA while empty returns `0xFEEDDEAD` with status SLVERR and sets the read-error flag and IRQS[2] (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/interface.adoc`, Access Results). After that read, STATUS reads 1 (`empty` set, `axil_mailbox.rdl`), ERROR_FLAGS reads 1 (the read-error flag), RIRQT reads 0, IRQS reads 4 (IRQS[2]), IRQP reads 0 and CTRL reads 0 with status SUCCESS (the flush bits "read back as zero", `interface.adoc`). A write of 1 to WIRQT and of 5 to IRQEN read back as written.
* WIRQT of mailbox 1 and mailbox 31 read their reset value 0 (`axil_mailbox.rdl`); after a write of 1 to mailbox 31 it reads back 1, mailbox 1 still reads 0 and mailbox 0 still reads 1. IRQEN of mailbox 1 and mailbox 31 read 0; after a write of 2 to mailbox 1 it reads back 2, mailbox 0 still reads 5 and mailbox 31 still reads 0.
* In the inbound aperture of pair 0, IRQEN and WIRQT read 0; after a write of 1 to its IRQEN it reads back 1 while the outbound IRQEN of mailbox 0 still reads 5 and the outbound WIRQT still reads 1.
* SMC_ATTRIBUTES at `0xC003_9200` is read as a low and a high 4-byte word, with an 8-byte read and a 32-bit slice taken instead when the high-word read does not return SUCCESS; the read returns SUCCESS with num_mailboxes 32 and mailbox_depth 2 (`hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/cpu_ctrl/cpu_ctrl.rdl`, SMC_ATTRIBUTES fields; `hw/sys/smc/doc/periphs.adoc`, mailbox rows of the peripheral parameter table).
* Measured and logged only: the response above the registers, a read at offsets 0x50 and 0x7F8 of mailbox 0 and a write at offset 0x50; the leaf logs the status and data of each access and does not compare them. The `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` vector at the end is logged and not compared.
* Mailbox data traffic, interrupt delivery and the full register set of mailboxes other than 0, 1 and 31 are out of scope.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event and for `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` to read 1, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns and require the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate open.
. Read the eight readable offsets of outbound mailbox 0 in order, write WIRQT = 1 and IRQEN = 5 and read each back.
. Read WIRQT of mailboxes 1 and 31, write 1 to mailbox 31, and read WIRQT of mailboxes 31, 1 and 0.
. Read IRQEN of mailboxes 1 and 31, write 2 to mailbox 1, and read IRQEN of mailboxes 1, 0 and 31.
. Read IRQEN and WIRQT of the inbound aperture of pair 0, write 1 to its IRQEN, read it back and read the outbound IRQEN and WIRQT of mailbox 0.
. Read both words of SMC_ATTRIBUTES and decode num_mailboxes and mailbox_depth.
. Read offsets 0x50 and 0x7F8 of mailbox 0, write offset 0x50 and log each status and data.
. Check that the reset-done wait, the primary-reset wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finished inside their bounds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MBX-OFFSET-MAP` |the ten offsets of outbound mailbox 0 answer as distinct registers with the values above and WIRQT and IRQEN read back what was written
|`CHK-MBX-STRIDE-WIRQT` |WIRQT of mailboxes 0, 1 and 31 at the 0x1000 stride holds its own value after a write to mailbox 31
|`CHK-MBX-IRQEN-INDEP` |IRQEN of mailboxes 0, 1 and 31 holds its own value after a write to mailbox 1
|`CHK-MBX-INBOUND-INDEP` |the inbound aperture of pair 0 at +0x800 keeps its IRQEN and WIRQT apart from those of the outbound aperture
|`CHK-MBX-SMC-ATTRIBUTES` |SMC_ATTRIBUTES returns SUCCESS with 32 mailboxes of depth 2
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the reset-done wait, the primary-reset wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finish inside their bounds, with the expected site names and access count, and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-161-smu_smc_mailbox_status_error_test]]
===== 161 `smu_smc_mailbox_status_error_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_mailbox_status_error_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_mailbox_status_error_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, 4-byte reads and writes with all four byte strobes); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable`; `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` (`rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o` on the bench top); bit 0 of the bench net `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` (the `smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` port of `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`) sampled on `clk_smu_i`; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images; the register addresses and the STATUS and IRQS bit masks come from the generated `smc_addr.h` and `axil_mailbox.h` of the register collateral

*Objective*: outbound mailbox 0 reports its FIFO occupancy in STATUS, answers an empty read and a full write with SLVERR and the matching error flags, raises and drops its interrupt lane on enable and acknowledge, and refuses writes to its read-only registers.

*Test Coverage*:

* Outbound mailbox 0 is at `smc_mailbox @ BASE_ADDR + 0x001_8000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`) with the register layout of `hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/regs/axil_mailbox.rdl`. The FIFO depth is 2 (`hw/sys/smc/doc/periphs.adoc`, mailbox rows of the peripheral parameter table). The outbound aperture writes one FIFO and reads the other, so words written through WRITE_DATA do not appear in its own READ_DATA FIFO (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/architecture.adoc`, bidirectional paragraph).
* Empty read: a read of READ_DATA while empty returns SLVERR with `0xFEEDDEAD`, ERROR_FLAGS then reads 1 (the read-error flag) and IRQS bit 2 reads 1 (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/interface.adoc`, Access Results; `architecture.adoc`, interrupt sources table).
* STATUS levels: with WIRQT at its reset value 0, one write of `0x11111111` to WRITE_DATA completes with SUCCESS and STATUS reads 5 (`empty` and `write_level_above_thresh`); a second write of `0x22222222` completes with SUCCESS and STATUS reads 7 (`full` added). STATUS "exposes live FIFO-empty/full and threshold conditions" (`architecture.adoc`).
* Full write: a third write completes with SLVERR, ERROR_FLAGS reads 2 (the write-error flag), IRQS bit 2 reads 1 and STATUS still reads 7 (`interface.adoc`, Access Results).
* Clear on read: a second read of ERROR_FLAGS returns 0 while IRQS bit 2 still reads 1, because reading ERROR_FLAGS "clears the detailed error flags, but does not acknowledge the shared IRQS error bit" (`architecture.adoc`).
* Error interrupt: after IRQEN is written with 4, IRQP bit 2 reads 1 and bit 0 of `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` rises within 2000 `clk_smu_i` edges; a write of 4 to IRQS acknowledges the bit (`woclr`, `axil_mailbox.rdl`), IRQS bit 2 reads 0 and the lane falls within 2000 edges. The lane is the interrupt of mailbox 0; the lane-to-mailbox mapping is proven by <<sec-069-smu_smc_mailbox_int_test>>.
* Flush: a write of 1 to CTRL flushes the write FIFO (`axil_mailbox.rdl`, `wflush`), after which STATUS reads 1 and IRQS bit 0 still reads 1, because the status bits latch until acknowledged (`architecture.adoc`).
* Read-only registers: writes to STATUS (0xF), READ_DATA (1), ERROR_FLAGS (3) and IRQP (7) complete with SLVERR, STATUS still reads 1, and ERROR_FLAGS and IRQP read 0 afterwards (`interface.adoc`, Access Results).
* The final lane value is logged and not compared. Threshold values above 0, the read-threshold path and the second aperture of the pair are out of scope.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event and for `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` to read 1, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns and require the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate open.
. Read READ_DATA while empty, then ERROR_FLAGS and IRQS, and compare status, data, flags and IRQS bit 2.
. Write `0x11111111` to WRITE_DATA and read STATUS.
. Write `0x22222222` to WRITE_DATA, read STATUS and compare both STATUS values and both write statuses.
. Write `0x33333333` to WRITE_DATA, then read ERROR_FLAGS, IRQS and STATUS and compare them.
. Read ERROR_FLAGS again and IRQS and compare them.
. Write 4 to IRQEN, read IRQP, wait for bit 0 of `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` to rise, write 4 to IRQS, read IRQS and wait for the lane to fall.
. Write 1 to CTRL, read STATUS and IRQS and compare STATUS and IRQS bit 0.
. Write 0xF to STATUS and read it back.
. Write READ_DATA, ERROR_FLAGS and IRQP, read ERROR_FLAGS and IRQP, and compare all seven results.
. Check that every JTAG2AXI poll and both lane waits finished inside their bounds, with the expected site names and access count.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MBX-ERR-READ-EMPTY` |an empty READ_DATA read answers SLVERR with `0xFEEDDEAD`, ERROR_FLAGS reads 1 and IRQS bit 2 reads 1
|`CHK-MBX-STATUS-LEVELS` |STATUS reads 5 after one push and 7 after two, each push completing with SUCCESS
|`CHK-MBX-ERR-WRITE-FULL` |a write to the full FIFO answers SLVERR, ERROR_FLAGS reads 2, IRQS bit 2 reads 1 and STATUS reads 7
|`CHK-MBX-ERR-CLEAR-ON-READ` |the second ERROR_FLAGS read returns 0 while IRQS bit 2 stays 1
|`CHK-MBX-ERR-IRQ-ACK` |IRQP bit 2 reads 1 after the enable, lane 0 rises, the IRQS acknowledge clears bit 2 and lane 0 falls
|`CHK-MBX-STATUS-FLUSH` |after the write-FIFO flush STATUS reads 1 and IRQS bit 0 is still set
|`CHK-MBX-RO-WRITES` |writes to STATUS, READ_DATA, ERROR_FLAGS and IRQP answer SLVERR and change neither STATUS nor the error flags nor IRQP
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |every JTAG2AXI poll and both lane waits finish inside their bounds, with the expected site names and access count, and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-162-smu_smc_mailbox_threshold_lane_test]]
===== 162 `smu_smc_mailbox_threshold_lane_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_mailbox_threshold_lane_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_mailbox_threshold_lane_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, 4-byte reads and writes with all four byte strobes, every access required to return SUCCESS); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable`; `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` (`rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o` on the bench top); the bench net `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` (the `smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` port of `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`) sampled on `clk_smu_i`; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images; the register addresses and the STATUS, IRQS and CTRL bit masks come from the generated `smc_addr.h` and `axil_mailbox.h` of the register collateral

*Objective*: the write-threshold interrupt of outbound mailbox 0 latches in IRQS and STATUS independently of IRQEN, reaches interrupt lane 0 only while enabled, returns to baseline on flush and acknowledge, and follows the clamped WIRQT threshold.

*Test Coverage*:

* Outbound mailbox 0 is at `smc_mailbox @ BASE_ADDR + 0x001_8000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`) with the register layout of `hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/regs/axil_mailbox.rdl`. The FIFO depth is 2 (`hw/sys/smc/doc/periphs.adoc`, mailbox rows of the peripheral parameter table). The rules are `hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/architecture.adoc`, Interrupts: IRQS bit 0 sets when the write FIFO occupancy is strictly greater than WIRQT, the status bits latch independently of IRQEN, `IRQP = IRQS & IRQEN`, each interrupt output is the OR of IRQP, writing ones to IRQS acknowledges, and WIRQT writes clamp to the depth minus 1.
* Latch with the enable clear: with IRQEN at its reset value and WIRQT at 0, one write of `0xA5A5A5A5` to WRITE_DATA leaves STATUS bit 2 and IRQS bit 0 at 1, IRQP at 0, and all 64 samples of `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` that follow read 0.
* Enable: a write of 1 to IRQEN makes IRQP read 1 and bit 0 of `ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` rise within 2000 `clk_smu_i` edges; a write of 0 makes the lane fall within 2000 edges while STATUS bit 2 still reads 1. The lane is the interrupt of mailbox 0; the lane-to-mailbox mapping is proven by <<sec-069-smu_smc_mailbox_int_test>>.
* Flush and acknowledge: with the lane high again, a write of 1 to CTRL flushes the write FIFO (`wflush`, `axil_mailbox.rdl`), after which STATUS reads 1, IRQS bit 0 still reads 1 and the lane reads 1 on all 64 samples that follow; a write of 1 to IRQS then makes IRQS read 0, the lane fall within 2000 edges and STATUS read 1.
* WIRQT clamp: after a write of 5 to WIRQT, which the unit clamps to 1, one queued word leaves the lane low on all 64 samples, STATUS bit 2 at 0 and IRQS bit 0 at 0; a second word makes the lane rise within 2000 edges with STATUS `full` and bit 2 at 1.
* The final clean-up writes a flush, an acknowledge, IRQEN = 0 and WIRQT = 0, and then STATUS reads 1 and lane 0 reads 0.
* Measured and logged only: an acknowledge written while two words are still queued, then the two IRQS reads that follow, the 256 lane samples after the write as runs of 0 and 1, and STATUS bit 2, which is required to still read 1. The architecture chapter says threshold status "can reassert if the occupancy condition remains true" without fixing when, so the leaf logs the lane and IRQS behaviour over that window and does not compare it.
* Read-threshold interrupts, the error interrupt and mailboxes other than 0 are out of scope.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event and for `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` to read 1, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns and require the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate open.
. Write `0xA5A5A5A5` to WRITE_DATA, read STATUS, IRQS and IRQP, take 64 lane samples and compare them.
. Write 1 to IRQEN, read IRQP and wait for lane 0 to rise.
. Write 0 to IRQEN, wait for lane 0 to fall and read STATUS.
. Write 1 to IRQEN, wait for lane 0 to rise, write 1 to CTRL, read STATUS and IRQS, take 64 lane samples, write 1 to IRQS, read IRQS, wait for the lane to fall and read STATUS.
. Write 5 to WIRQT and one word to WRITE_DATA, take 64 lane samples, read STATUS and IRQS, write a second word, wait for the lane to rise and read STATUS.
. Write 1 to IRQS with two words queued, take 256 lane samples, read IRQS twice and STATUS, and log the lane runs.
. Write 1 to CTRL and to IRQS, write 0 to IRQEN and WIRQT, read STATUS, sample the lane and compare.
. Check that every JTAG2AXI poll and the five lane waits finished inside their bounds, with the expected site names.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MBX-WT-LATCH-GATED` |with IRQEN at 0 and one word queued IRQS bit 0 and STATUS bit 2 read 1 and lane 0 stays low
|`CHK-MBX-IRQP-GATING` |IRQP reads 0 with IRQEN at 0 and 1 after IRQEN is set, and lane 0 rises
|`CHK-MBX-WT-ENABLE-DROP` |clearing IRQEN drops lane 0 while STATUS bit 2 stays 1
|`CHK-MBX-WT-ACK-BASELINE` |the flush empties STATUS, IRQS stays latched with the lane high until the write-one acknowledge drops lane 0 and clears IRQS
|`CHK-MBX-WT-CLAMP` |WIRQT = 5 acts as a threshold of 1: one word raises no interrupt, two words raise lane 0, and the clean-up returns STATUS to 1 with the lane low
|`CHK-MBX-WT-REASSERT-LOG` |an acknowledge with the occupancy still above WIRQT leaves STATUS bit 2 at 1, and the IRQS reads and lane runs are logged
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |every JTAG2AXI poll and the five lane waits finish inside their bounds, with the expected site names, and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-163-smu_smc_reset_unit_state_test]]
===== 163 `smu_smc_reset_unit_state_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_reset_unit_state_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_reset_unit_state_test_seq` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (the primary TAP and the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge, 4-byte read-only accesses); bench observation `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable`; the idle bench inputs `tb_cfg_flr_pf_active` (drives `cfg_flr_pf_active_i`) and `tb_cool_reset_pin` (drives the cool-reset pin), both required to read 0; bench observations `tb_isolate_req`, `tb_skip_mem_repair` and `tb_sync_irq` of `isolate_req_o`, `skip_mem_repair_o` and `sync_irq_o`, sampled on `clk_smu_i`; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+skip_fuse_sense` with the default SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and the `+rom_hex`, `+smc_efuse_hex`, `+sep_efuse_hex`, `+sep_boot_rom_hex`, `+sep_itcm_hex` and `+sep_dtcm_hex` hex images; the register addresses and reset values the leaf compares come from the generated `smc_addr.h` and `reset_unit.h` of the SMC register collateral

*Objective*: after boot the SMC reset unit registers read their documented reset values and the isolation, skip-repair and sync outputs are idle.

*Test Coverage*:

* The block is `smc_reset_unit @ BASE_ADDR + 0x000_2000` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/smc.rdl`) and every offset and reset value below is the RDL's (`hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/reset_unit/reset_unit.rdl`); all registers are read with 4-byte accesses and nothing is written.
* SS_CONFIG at +0x20, SS_CONFIG_LOCK at +0x24, SS_COLD_RESET_N at +0x40, SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK at +0x70 and SS_FORCE_TO_REF_CLK at +0x80 read 0, and SS_WARM_RESET_N at +0x44 reads `0xFFFFFFFF`.
* SS_CONFIG_HOLD at +0x48, SS_SRAM_HOLD at +0x4C, SS_CRITICAL_HOLD at +0x50 and SS_DEBUG_HOLD at +0x54 read 0.
* ISOLATE_REQ_REG at +0xB0, ISOLATE_REQ_PINEN_REG at +0xB4, ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG at +0xB8, ISOLATE_REQ_SMCEN_REG at +0xBC, ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_COUNTER_VALUE at +0xC4, ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_RESET_COUNTER_VALUE at +0xC8 and SYNC_REG at +0xA8 read 0.
* ISOLATE_REQ_VIS at +0xC0 reads `cool_reset_n_i` (bit 4) and `cool_reset_n_o` (bit 8) at 1 with the cool-reset and FLR inputs idle.
* With those inputs idle, `isolate_req_o` reads `0x0` as the OR of three requests that are all clear (`hw/sys/smc/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Isolation Controls), `skip_mem_repair_o` reads 0 as the OR of the synchronized isolation pin and the FLR isolation latch (`clk_rst.adoc`, Timing and Recovery) and `sync_irq_o` reads 0 as `SYNC_REG.sync` (`hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`, `isolate_req_o`, `skip_mem_repair_o` and `sync_irq_o` rows).
* Measured and logged only: the `isolate_req_pin` field, bit 0 of ISOLATE_REQ_VIS. Bit 0 reads the idle pin level 0, which differs from the RDL reset value 1 of this hardware-written field; logged, not compared. The leaf compares only bits 4 and 8.
* Writes, the FLR and cool-reset sequences, the SS_RESET_COMPLETE register and the reset behaviour behind the registers are out of scope: the leaf proves reset state and idle outputs only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event, reset the TAP, require the IDCODE the bench TAP returns, require the `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` gate open and require `tb_cfg_flr_pf_active` and `tb_cool_reset_pin` at 0.
. Read SS_CONFIG, SS_CONFIG_LOCK, SS_COLD_RESET_N, SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK, SS_FORCE_TO_REF_CLK and SS_WARM_RESET_N and compare them with their reset values.
. Read the four hold registers and compare them with their reset values.
. Read the four isolation registers, the two FLR counter registers and SYNC_REG and compare them with their reset values.
. Read ISOLATE_REQ_VIS, log bit 0 and compare bits 4 and 8.
. Sample `tb_isolate_req`, `tb_skip_mem_repair` and `tb_sync_irq` on a `clk_smu_i` edge and compare them with 0.
. Check that the reset-done wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finished inside their bounds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BOOT-RSTUNIT-CONFIG` |SS_CONFIG, the two locks, SS_COLD_RESET_N and SS_FORCE_TO_REF_CLK read 0 and SS_WARM_RESET_N reads `0xFFFFFFFF`
|`CHK-BOOT-RSTUNIT-HOLDS` |the four hold registers read 0
|`CHK-BOOT-RSTUNIT-ISOLATION` |the four isolation registers, the two FLR counter registers and SYNC_REG read 0
|`CHK-BOOT-RSTUNIT-VIS` |ISOLATE_REQ_VIS bits 4 and 8 read 1
|`CHK-BOOT-RSTUNIT-IDLE-OUTPUTS` |`isolate_req_o`, `skip_mem_repair_o` and `sync_irq_o` are 0 with the pins idle
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the reset-done wait and every JTAG2AXI poll finish inside their bounds and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-164-smu_smc_sram_init_sequence_test]]
===== 164 `smu_smc_sram_init_sequence_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_sram_init_sequence_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; the sequence `smu_smc_sram_init_sequence_test_seq` and the sampler class `smu_smc_sram_init_sampler` in `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`)

*BFM / Model*: the sampler, armed ahead of the shared bring-up, which reads `smc_init_mem_done_o`, the bench scratchpad write counter `smc_scratch_write_count_dv_o` (`u_smc_cpu_mem_dv` under `u_dut`) and the bench ROM read counter `smc_rom_read_count_o` (counts ROM read requests) on every `clk_smu_i` edge; stimulus `rst_cold_ni` for the second cold boot, held for 600 `clk_ref_i` edges, with `rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o` read during it; `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl` run mode; `+rom_hex` and `+smc_rom_hex` set to the minimal-pass SMC ROM image, `+skip_fuse_sense` with the SMC and SEP shadow preloads, and no `+smc_scratch_ram_hex`, so the wrapper leaves `smc_disable_sram_auto_init_i` low

*Objective*: `smc_init_mem_done_o` rises once per cold boot after the scratchpad zero-fill has written, before the SMC fetches from the ROM, and rises again after a second cold reset.

*Test Coverage*:

* The scratchpad zero-fill sequencer writes zeros across the scratchpad banks and holds the cores in reset until initialization completes, `smc_disable_sram_auto_init_i` bypasses it, and `smc_init_mem_done_o` reports completion or bypass (`hw/sys/smc/doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset and Boot; `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`, `smc_init_mem_done_o` and `smc_disable_sram_auto_init_i` rows). The leaf requires that no scratchpad RAM image is preloaded so the automatic initialization is the path that runs.
* First boot: `smc_init_mem_done_o` has exactly one rising edge, the scratchpad write count is above 0 at that edge and unchanged 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles later, and the pin reads 1.
* The ROM read counter reads 0 at the rising edge and its first increment falls on a later cycle, because the cores are held in reset until initialization completes (`cpu.adoc`).
* Second boot: `rst_cold_ni` is held low for 600 `clk_ref_i` edges and released; `smc_init_mem_done_o` then has a second rising edge, the pin reads 1 and the scratchpad write count at that edge exceeds the count read during the reset.
* Measured and logged only: the level of `smc_init_mem_done_o`, the level of `rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o` and the scratchpad write count during the second reset, and the cycle numbers of both rises and of the first ROM read. The level of `smc_init_mem_done_o` while the cold reset is asserted is logged and not compared.
* The bypass path with `smc_disable_sram_auto_init_i` high, memory repair and the scratchpad contents are out of scope: the leaf checks the indication and its order, not the data written.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the reset-done event and require that no `+smc_scratch_ram_hex` image is set.
. Wait for the first rise of `smc_init_mem_done_o` and 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles after it, then compare the rise count, the write count at the rise and 64 cycles later, and the pin level.
. Wait for the first ROM read and compare the ROM read counter at the rise with 0 and the cycle order of the rise and the first read.
. Drive `rst_cold_ni` low for 600 `clk_ref_i` edges, log the done level, the primary reset level and the write count, release it and wait for the second rise.
. Compare the rise count, the write count at the second rise against the count read in reset, and the pin level; stop the sampler.
. Check that the sampler and every wait finished inside their bounds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BOOT-INIT-DONE-ONCE` |`smc_init_mem_done_o` rises once on the first cold boot, with scratchpad writes above 0 at the rise and none in the next 64 `clk_smu_i` cycles
|`CHK-BOOT-INIT-BEFORE-FETCH` |the ROM read counter is 0 at the done rise and first increments after it
|`CHK-BOOT-INIT-REARM` |after a second cold reset `smc_init_mem_done_o` rises a second time with a scratchpad write count above the count read during that reset
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the sampler and the done-rise and ROM-read waits finish inside their bounds and no wait record shows an expiry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
==== SMU_WRAPPER

`--dut smu` is the wrapper bench, and `testlists/all.toml` enrols the whole
SMU regression on it. The entries in this section are the wrapper-native
names: the elaboration leaf, the probe leaf and the SEP firmware set.

Each entry describes the test body and its sequence under
`cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`, including what the leaf does not prove.

Two things are worth reading before the entries:

* *The verdict is usually the firmware's.* Most of these boot a real SEP DV
  image that parks in a named terminal loop, and the run is classified by which
  loop PC the SEP settles on. `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/sep_terminal_loop_seq.py`
  resolves those loop addresses from the staged `.sym`, refuses a run whose
  ITCM image and symbol table come from different firmwares, and reports a fail
  loop the linker garbage-collected as a path *not covered* rather than as
  passed. Its tokens are `+++CHK-<NAME>-FRONTDOOR+++` and
  `+++CHK-<NAME>-VERDICT+++`, where `<NAME>` is the sequence's own `NAME`
  upper-cased, so a token such as `CHK-SEP_AES-VERDICT` keeps the underscore of
  the sequence name.
* *`smu_sep_smoke_test` and `smu_smc_smoke_test` are not listed again here.*
  <<sec-066-smu_sep_smoke_test>> and <<sec-072-smu_smc_smoke_test>> describe the
  `cocotb_wrapper/tests/` bodies those names enrol, which are the only bodies of
  those names in the tree.

[[sec-078-smu_wrapper_elaboration_test]]
===== 78 `smu_wrapper_elaboration_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_wrapper_elaboration_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `elaboration` firmware mode, so no CPU image is staged

*Objective*: SMU_ALL_001: the SEP=1 wrapper composes SMC+SEP+DTP+xbar on one clock/reset domain and exports the SEP lifecycle state.

*Test Coverage*:

* `+expected_sep=1` selects `_run_smu_all_001`, the DV-CARD SMU_ALL_001 path; this is the only leaf that runs it.
* The S1 setup is a driven observation, not a sampled level: the sequence deasserts `powergood_i` and requires `obs_powergood_stable_o` to fall after a proven high and to rise again afterwards, so a composition that never sees power-good deasserted fails the bounded wait.
* Hierarchical clock identity is sampled at the SMC, SEP, DTP and xbar levels and each must equal the top-level `clk_smu` level on the same edge.
* The three clock domains (`clk_smu`, telemetry/reference, SEP watchdog) must have distinct periods, and their level transitions counted over a fixed 240 ns window must differ accordingly -- a coincidence is ruled out by the period check, not assumed.
* `lc_state_o` at the SMU boundary is compared against the SEP-sourced lifecycle value, so the export is a compare and not a tie-off read.
* S3 is scoped: `port_group=smu_axi,lifecycle` is proven; `jtag,xtrig` is logged explicitly as `NOT-PROVEN` because this step drives neither.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive a `powergood_i` deassertion and observe `obs_powergood_stable_o` go 1 -> 0 -> 1 around it, then wait for `rst_cold_n_o` to release.
. Assert `sep_enabled_o == 1` -- this leaf refuses to run against a `no_sep` build.
. Sample the SMC/SEP/DTP/xbar hierarchical clock levels against the top-level clock on the same edges.
. Observe the primary SMC reset through a cold assert and a cold deassert.
. Count level transitions on three DUT clock nodes over 240 ns and compare them against the configured periods, which must themselves be distinct.
. Read the SEP lifecycle value and compare `lc_state_o` against it.
. Check the timeout-path inventory carries a finite bound and a last-state diagnostic, then run the non-vacuity fence.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-COMPOSE-BLOCKS-S1` |`sep=1`, blocks `smc+sep+dtp+xbar` present, hierarchical clock identity holds at every sampled level
|`CHK-SMU-COMPOSE-BLOCKS-S2` |`clk_smu` domain with `rst_primary_smc_clk_no`: last sampled clock levels and the primary reset agree
|`CHK-SMU-COMPOSE-BLOCKS-S3` |`port_group=smu_axi,lifecycle` proven; `port_group=jtag,xtrig` is logged `NOT-PROVEN` in the same step
|`CHK-SMU-PORT-CLK-RST-S1` |cold assert and cold deassert both observed on `rst_primary_smc`
|`CHK-SMU-PORT-CLK-RST-S3` |telemetry and SEP-watchdog clocks are separate from `clk_smu_i`: distinct periods and distinct 240 ns transition counts
|`CHK-SMU-SEP-PARAM-S1` |`sep=1`, xbar present, and `lc_state_o` matches the SEP-sourced lifecycle value
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the S8 timeout inventory carries a finite bound and a last-state diagnostic on every path
|`CHK-NONVAC` |step ordering, simulation-time advance and the DUT comparison floor all hold
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw_boot`, `elaboration`.

'''''
[[sec-079-smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test]]
===== 79 `smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (`OcahJtagMasterDriver` on the primary TAP); the SEP runs the `hello_world` DV image

*Objective*: The primary TAP answers on the production wrapper while the SEP is executing, and TAP access does not disturb it.

*Test Coverage*:

* S1 IDCODE matches the RTL default and TDO is resolved, so the TAP answers rather than reading back as an unresolved 0.
* S2 BYPASS is a one-bit register: over an 8-bit scan TDO returns the pattern shifted up one bit. IDCODE alone can be satisfied by a stuck chain; the one-bit delay cannot.
* S3 both results hold at a second TCK period, so neither is tied to one clock ratio against `clk_smu`.
* S4 the SEP retire count still advances across the TAP accesses -- the part specific to this profile.
* S5 the IC_RESET override bits are clear, which every other wrapper leaf rests on: an asserted override holds SMC cold/fuse reset and the SEP never fetches.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the primary TAP through `wrapper_jtag` and read IDCODE, checking the value and that TDO is resolved.
. Shift an 8-bit pattern through BYPASS and compare TDO against the pattern delayed by exactly one bit.
. Repeat both at a second TCK period.
. Sample the SEP retire count before and after the TAP accesses and require it to have advanced.
. Read back the IC_RESET override TDR and require the SMC and SEP override bits to be clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-WRAPPER-PTAP-IDCODE` |IDCODE read at the sampled TCK period matches the RTL default and TDO is resolved
|`CHK-WRAPPER-PTAP-BYPASS` |one-bit register at each TCK period; a stuck chain cannot produce the TDI delay
|`CHK-WRAPPER-PTAP-SEP-LIVE` |the SEP retire count advanced across the TAP accesses
|`CHK-WRAPPER-IC-RESET-CLEAR` |SMC override bits and the SEP override read back clear, so the base test's TAP reset walk really does clear the TDR
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_probe`.

'''''
[[sec-080-smu_sep_boot_health_test]]
===== 80 `smu_sep_boot_health_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_boot_health_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_boot_health` (on-chip verdict), classified through the SEP trace probes

*Objective*: The SEP boots the real DV boot-health image and parks in its own pass loop, with the boot order observed.

*Test Coverage*:

* Runs the SEP DV boot-health image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_boot_health`, not a minimal freestanding smoke.
* The verdict is the firmware's: it parks in one of two named terminal loops and the run is classified by which loop PC the SEP settles on.
* Loop addresses come from the staged `.sym`, so the contract survives a firmware relink.
* `boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` first-seen ordering is asserted, so a pass loop reached without a real boot fails.
* SEP-local cold scratch7 (`0x1080_2038`) marker observation is out of scope here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load the staged symbol table and resolve the pass and fail loop addresses; a pass loop outside the ICCM window is a failure.
. Run the SEP and sample `sep_trace_valid_o` / `sep_pc_o` each `clk_smu` edge, recording first boot-ROM fetch, first ICCM execution and first pass-loop PC.
. Stop at the first terminal loop entered and classify the run by that PC.
. Assert the boot-ROM, ICCM and ordering conditions, then emit the evidence tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-BOOT-HEALTH-FRONTDOOR` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, and both boot-ROM and ICCM execution were seen
|`CHK-SEP-BOOT-HEALTH-VERDICT` |the pass loop was reached and no fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` first-seen ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_BOOT_OK`, `SEP_REAL_FW_PASS_LOOP_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-081-smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test]]
===== 81 `smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/rom_no_tcm_preload_mem_init` running from boot ROM with `+sep_no_tcm_preload`; verdict through the SEP trace probes and the STDOUT mailbox

*Objective*: The SEP loads its own ICCM from boot ROM and executes it, with no testbench TCM preload.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every other real-firmware anchor here has its ICCM/DCCM placed by the testbench backdoor; that backdoor hides the CPU loading its own TCM. This leaf is the one that closes the gap.
* `+sep_no_tcm_preload` leaves the TCM holding zeroes when the CPU starts, so ICCM execution is only reachable if the firmware's own DMA put code there.
* The image writes and reads back DCCM from ROM-fetched code, stages a routine into SEP SRAM as raw instruction words, opens the secure-DMA range, disables local-alias remap, DMAs SRAM to ICCM, and jumps to the copy.
* The ICCM PC is checked separately from the mailbox verdict: reaching the DMA target is what distinguishes a real load from a firmware that reported PASS some other way.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run with `+sep_no_tcm_preload` so no ICCM/DCCM image is backdoored.
. Observe boot-ROM execution, then the DMA-done marker, then a retired PC inside the ICCM window.
. Read the STDOUT mailbox for `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`.
. Assert all three in order before emitting the evidence tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-ROM-TCM-LOAD` |ROM execution, then `dma_done`, then ICCM execution at the DMA target, then `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`; the TCM was zero at reset
|`CHK-SEP-ROM-TCM-NONVAC` |no TB TCM preload was applied, so ICCM execution is only possible because the firmware's own DMA put code there
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_ROM_TCM_LOAD_OK`, `SEP_SELF_LOADED_ICCM_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-082-smu_sep_sanity_test]]
===== 82 `smu_sep_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_sanity` (HMAC + KMAC KAT, on-chip verdict); STDOUT mailbox handshake at `0x8000_0000`

*Objective*: The SEP runs SHA-256 (HMAC) and SHA3-256 (KMAC) known-answer vectors on-chip and reports PASS through the mailbox.

*Test Coverage*:

* The firmware programs its own SEP outbound filter, runs both KATs, then reports `TEST_MAGIC0 -> TEST_MAGIC_PASS` per `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/include/tb.h`.
* The verdict is the firmware's, not the testbench's: a PASS means the SEP compared both digests against the NIST vectors on-chip. The TB only observes.
* Five `STAGE_BEACON` words (`0xB1B0B0B_<id>`) give stage-level progress -- main entered, filter programmed, HMAC entered, HMAC done / KMAC entered, KMAC done -- and all five are required.
* Beacon 0 follows `sep_outbound_filter_init()`: a store ahead of it faults against the `BLOCK_BY_DEFAULT=1` outbound filter and kills the image, so the beacon's absence is a real failure, not a missing print.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Boot the image and watch the SEP trace for ICCM execution.
. Collect the five stage beacons in order as the firmware publishes them.
. Read the STDOUT mailbox and require `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`.
. Assert the `iccm exec < beacon 1..4 < PASS magic` ordering before emitting the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-SANITY-STAGES` |all five stage beacons were reached in the SEP: the filter, HMAC and KMAC stages were each entered and left
|`CHK-SEP-SANITY-KAT` |the firmware wrote `TEST_MAGIC_PASS` -- both known-answer vectors matched on-chip
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`iccm exec < beacon 1..4 < PASS magic` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_SANITY_OK`, `SEP_HMAC_KMAC_KAT_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-083-smu_sep_modules_test]]
===== 83 `smu_sep_modules_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_modules_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_modules` (on-chip verdict); OCAH-local ESRC noise driver under `+esrc_noise_force`

*Objective*: The SEP module matrix -- AES, HMAC and KMAC in this build -- clears on-chip, with the failing module named.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image walks the SEP module touch-points its stage mask enables and parks in a per-stage terminal loop, so a failure names the module rather than reporting "firmware did not pass".
* The stage mask is compile-time and the linker garbage-collects loops for disabled stages, so the watch set is derived from the symbol table: a stage that is not built has no loop symbol, and a stage that is built must have one.
* `+esrc_noise_force` supplies raw noise the ring oscillators cannot generate under Verilator.
* This is a matrix over module reachability, not a cryptographic depth test; the dedicated AES vector is `smu_sep_aes_test`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start the ESRC noise driver.
. Resolve the per-stage pass and fail loop symbols from the staged `.sym`, treating an absent symbol as a stage not covered rather than a stage passed.
. Run the SEP and classify by the terminal loop PC reached.
. Assert the boot-ROM / ICCM / ordering conditions and emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-MODULES-FRONTDOOR` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`CHK-SEP-MODULES-MATRIX` |every enabled stage cleared on-chip; the pass loop was reached and no per-stage fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_MODULES_OK`, `SEP_MODULE_MATRIX_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-084-smu_sep_aes_test]]
===== 84 `smu_sep_aes_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_aes_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_aes` (on-chip KAT verdict); OCAH-local ESRC noise driver under `+esrc_noise_force`

*Objective*: One AES-128 ECB known-answer vector is encrypted and compared on-chip, with the alert status checked before the image parks.

*Test Coverage*:

* The same AES-128 ECB known-answer vector the AES stage of `smu_sep_modules_test` runs -- `sep_smu_aes` and `sep_smu_modules` share the key, plaintext and expected ciphertext -- on a dedicated image whose only subject is AES. What this leaf adds is the cipher in isolation from the HMAC and KMAC stages and the alert-status check before the image parks, not a second vector.
* AES masking reseeds its PRNG from crypto-EDN, so the image brings the entropy stack up first and parks in a *distinct* fail loop if that does not complete -- the prerequisite and the cipher stay separately attributable.
* The sequence refuses to run without `+esrc_noise_force`: without driven noise the image stalls at the ESRC boot gate and never reaches the cipher, which would otherwise look like an AES failure.
* The cycle budget clears the ESRC boot health-test window (2048 samples at div64) with margin; the KAT itself is a few thousand cycles.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert `+esrc_noise_force` was passed, then start the ESRC noise driver.
. Resolve `smu_sep_aes_pass_loop`, `smu_sep_aes_fail_loop` and `smu_sep_aes_fail_entropy_loop` from the staged `.sym`.
. Run the SEP and classify by the terminal loop PC reached.
. Assert the front-door and ordering conditions and emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_AES-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`+CHK-SEP_AES-VERDICT+` |the pass loop was reached and neither the AES nor the entropy fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_AES_OK`, `SEP_AES_ECB128_KAT_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-085-smu_sep_otbn_test]]
===== 85 `smu_sep_otbn_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_otbn_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_otbn`, classified through the SEP trace probes

*Objective*: The OTBN register aperture is reachable over the SEP-internal fabric -- a reachability claim, not an OTBN functional one.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image writes INTR_ENABLE, INTR_STATE, ERR_BITS, INSN_CNT and LOAD_CHECKSUM, reads INTR_ENABLE back, and parks in its pass loop only when the readback equals the value written.
* OTBN is at `0x1090_0000`, inside the SEP's own peripheral region, so these accesses travel the SEP-internal fabric. The outbound filter is not on this path: its only open window is the 256-byte STDOUT mailbox, and a write behind it would have been blocked rather than completed.
* *What this proves*: reaching the pass loop means no access took a store fault -- `crt0` installs a trap handler that parks in `_finish` instead -- and that the INTR_ENABLE write arrived and stuck, because the image compares its own readback and branches to the fail loop on a mismatch. That closes the default-slave case, where a write is answered without reaching the register.
* *What it does not prove*: nothing about OTBN functionality and nothing about the SEP outbound fabric. There is no IMEM/DMEM load and no EXECUTE; the other four writes are covered only by the absence of a fault; no transaction is counted at the OTBN aperture itself.
* This image needs no entropy -- it touches neither AES masking nor the Key Manager.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Resolve the pass and fail loop symbols from the staged `.sym`.
. Run the SEP and classify by the terminal loop PC reached.
. Assert the front-door and ordering conditions and emit the token.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_OTBN-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`+CHK-SEP_OTBN-VERDICT+` |the pass loop was reached -- i.e. no CSR access faulted and the firmware's own INTR_ENABLE readback matched -- and no fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_OTBN_CSR_REACHABLE`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-086-smu_sep_entropy_test]]
===== 86 `smu_sep_entropy_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_entropy_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_entropy_bringup`; OCAH-local ESRC noise driver under `+esrc_noise_force`

*Objective*: The SEP firmware brings up ESRC -> CSRNG -> EDN and entropy is proven to flow, not merely to have been programmed.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image programs the stack through the driver the SEP DV tree already ships, in the order that driver documents.
* Programming the stack is *not* the claim: a firmware that wrote the registers into a dead block would reach its pass loop just the same.
* The claim is checked at four points down the chain -- `esrc_noise_active` (lane-0's `dcor.noise_i` tracks the driven bit, so the force took), `drbg_seed_valid` (ESRC produced a seed the health tests accepted), `drbg_es_ack` (CSRNG consumed it) and `drbg_genbits_vld` (the CTR_DRBG produced genbits).
* Bit-exact prediction of the genbits is not attempted; that needs the golden chain in `hw/sys/sep/dv` and belongs there.
* `smu_sep_aes_test` depends on this entropy stack coming up; this leaf is where the stack itself is proven.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start the ESRC noise driver and confirm lane-0's `dcor.noise_i` tracks the driven bit.
. Run the firmware bring-up and observe the PHASE-A / generators / PHASE-B programming order.
. Observe `drbg_seed_valid`, then `drbg_es_ack`, then `drbg_genbits_vld`.
. Require the firmware to reach its pass loop, then emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-ENTROPY-FW-BRINGUP` |the firmware programmed ESRC/CSRNG/EDN in the documented PHASE-A / generators / PHASE-B order and reached its pass loop
|`CHK-SEP-ENTROPY-FLOWS` |forced noise reached `dcor.noise_i`, ESRC produced an accepted seed, CSRNG consumed it and the CTR_DRBG produced genbits -- the chain runs, not just its registers
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_ENTROPY_FW_BRINGUP_OK`, `SEP_ENTROPY_CHAIN_FLOWS_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_entropy`.

'''''
[[sec-087-smu_sep_efuse_test]]
===== 87 `smu_sep_efuse_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_efuse_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_efuse` (on-chip verdict), classified by the terminal-loop PC the SEP settles on; loop addresses come from the staged `.sym`, so the contract survives a relink

*Objective*: The SEP-side eFuse control and external-shim CSR read/write path clears on-chip.

*Test Coverage*:

* The firmware exercises the SEP eFuse control and external-shim CSR path and parks in its own pass or fail loop.
* The verdict is the firmware's; the testbench only watches which loop the SEP settles on.
* This is the SEP-side eFuse CSR path. It carries no claim about SMC eFuse, OTP burn or lifecycle decode -- those are `smu_sep_lcc_*`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Require the staged ITCM image and the `.sym` to name the same firmware -- sibling images share a crt0 layout, so loop addresses collide and a mismatched pair would give a confident, wrong PASS.
. Resolve the pass loop and the fail loops (`efuse`) from the symbol table; a fail symbol the linker dropped is reported as a path not covered, not as passed.
. Run the SEP, sampling `sep_trace_valid_o` / `sep_pc_o` each `clk_smu` edge, and stop at the first terminal loop entered.
. Assert the boot-ROM, ICCM and `boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering conditions, then emit the evidence tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_SEP_EFUSE-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, and both boot-ROM and ICCM execution were seen
|`+CHK-SEP_SEP_EFUSE-VERDICT+` |the pass loop was reached and no fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_EFUSE_OK`, `SEP_EFUSE_CSR_PATH_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
